-- 14 of 600 --

1. Introduction
When NC programming is carried out using a CAM system, specific CAD functionalities are
required for the preparation of work.
With
hyperCAD®-S, OPEN MIND has developed its own CAD system that is perfectly suited
to
hyperMILL® and is designed to fully meet the requirements of CAM users. Whether
users are working with meshes, faces or solids to create precise components and tools –
hyperCAD®-S always provides the right answer.
The software is a powerful 64-bit software application and the CAD platform for
hyperMILL®
from OPEN MIND. It is characterised by the following state-of-the-art features:
• Own CAD kernel with special CAM objects
• Modern database technologies
• Real-time rendering
• Touch operation
• Simultaneous work with multiple screens
• Dockable and freely positionable user interface
• Independent, parallel working in different project files thanks to multi-core support and
multithreading
• Password protection and document encryption
• Multiple filters available, e.g., colour, layer, geometric and user-defined attributes and
system properties
Installation Please follow the installation information for installing the software. If you have any ques-
tions concerning the installation, please contact Support first.
Hardware requirements
The software requires a 64-bit operating system and uses OpenGL. For up-to-date in-
formation about hardware requirements and supported operating systems, please visit
www.openmind-tech.com and go to ‘Support > Software requirements’ in the ‘Service’ area
of our website.
Licensing
The software is copy-protected via hardware. A dongle and an activation code are provided
for operating the system. Each licence is unique. Protect your licence against loss.
For more information, please refer to the installation and licensing information on the web-
site and in the
hyperMILL® software documentation.
Select system of units
Select a system of units in the
hyperMILL® Settings Wizard. The setting is also valid for the
software. Set the Metric option for the unit of measure in millimetres. Set the Inch option for
the unit of measure in inches.

omAppSettingsX64.exe software under ...\Info Center\.... This means that the association
only applies to OPEN MIND software. The association for other software on the computer
remains unaffected.

2. User interface
You can start each document as a separate process. The application is adapted to the use
of wide screen formats and for working with multiple screens. You can also place variable
areas outside of the program window, that is, you can move them to another screen. When
you exit a document the last window positions are saved.
The user interface is composed of the following elements:
7	1 23
4
5
6
8
9

5
Title bar
The title bar 1 	displays the name of the currently opened document. To hide the title bar,
press the F11 key.
Menu bar
The software's commands are accessible from the menu bar 2 	. The menu bar is static and
cannot be changed.
Brief information
Brief information on commands at positions where the cursor rests or on entries required
within a command – on the right in the menu bar 9 	.
Progress bar
Progress is displayed for processes that require considerable processing power 10 	and
information is provided about the current action within the process (if possible).
Toolbars
Toolbars 3 	are used to call software commands. Click the icon with the left mouse button.
The user can create own toolbars, and the content of toolbars can be modified.
hyperCAD®-S
5

-- 19 of 600 --

Dialogues
In numerous commands, data is entered in a dialogue.
Graphics area
The graphics area 4 	allows you to interact with the entities of a document as geometric
representations.
Toolbars in the graphics area
The software's commands are accessible from the menu bar. Toolbars 5 	are fixed in their
position. The contents of a toolbar can be changed.
Context menu
Context menus 6 	provide a selection of commands that are relevant for the work that is
currently being carried out, also in areas for entering text and values. To open a context
menu, briefly press the right mouse button.
Depending on the context, you can double-click with the left mouse button in the graphics
area within a command or selection to automatically jump to the next input that is appropri-
ate for the sequence. The Continue command or another command highlighted in bold on
the context menu indicates this option. If the Apply command is highlighted in bold, the
entries will be applied and the calculation will be performed.
Click OK or Exit to exit the command. Recent commands allows you to call up a command
from a list of most recently used commands. The length of this list can be controlled using
the Max. recent commands option under File → Options → Options / properties → User
interface.
Press the ENTER key to recall the previous command.
Tool tabs
Tool tabs 7 	are used to call commands via icons. If the content of a tab is partly hidden,
you can scroll through all of the available commands with the scroll wheel while holding
down the ALT key at the same time.
Tab tools
Tab tools 8 	are used for structural information, for filtering, for displaying messages and
attributes and for the
hyperMILL® browser.

Start screen In the ‘Homescreen’ start screen, current information on
hyperMILL® and
hyperCAD®-S is
offered in the news area. A document can be opened or a new document can be created –
as with Open and New. The most recently used documents are displayed.
The font size of the news text can be changed usingCTRL and the scroll wheel. The start
screen can be switched on and off using the keyboard shortcut ALT+h. Use + or x to show
or hide the news area. The news area is not displayed if there is no news in the selected
language or no access to the Internet is available. Press F5 to refresh the start screen.

Graphics area The graphics area allows you to interact with the entities of a document as geometric
representations.
Edges and boundaries
Face boundaries represent the borders of a face and are indicated by a dashed line when
the mouse is positioned within the face a short distance away from the border. Borders
hyperCAD®-S
6

-- 20 of 600 --

within solids are edges and are indicated by a thin solid line when the mouse is positioned
directly on the edge or in close proximity to it.
Manipulator
Within various commands, you can use the manipulator to edit input interactively. To do
this, click the manipulator with the left mouse button and move it to the desired location
while holding down the mouse button. The position at which you click influences the range
of motion. If you select an axis, the manipulator moves along the direction of this axis A 	. If
you select an arc, the manipulator is rotated around the axis B 	. If you choose an origin, the
manipulator can be freely positioned C 	.
To ‘latch’ precisely to a geometrically significant position of an entity, activate the Snap
for manipulator / handles option. Select the option in the context menu under Selection or
preset in theSnap selection filter using the Use for manipulator / handles option.
Distances or positions can optionally be selected only in a specified step size. Enable the
Grid for manipulator / handles option under Options / properties and specify the step sizes.

B
C

Within commands, handles D 	are provided with which changes can be made interactively.
Click a handle with the left mouse button and move the handle while holding down the
mouse button until the required size is reached.

Mini dialogue boxes
A mini dialogue box is displayed near to the entity. Enter the value here rather than in the
command dialogue. The context menu (right-click to open) provides a selection of options
for the respective command that allow you to switch between different input options, such
as between the angle of an arc and its radius.
Switch to the mini dialogue box using the TAB key If the input field is not active for an entry,
the value is displayed rounded. During an input, a value is displayed fully accurately.
Angles can be input as deg:min:sec or deg min sec (separated by spaces) and will then be
converted to the selected format.
Tooltip
The entity ID, type and geometric attributes, for example, can be displayed for dynamically
highlighted entities. A workplane can be identified via its name. Switching on is possible
under Options / properties, configuring is possible under File → Options → Tooltip content.
An error message can also be shown in a tooltip when directly selecting entities or positions
during dimensioning, for example.

Default values are automatically converted between metric and inch when the system of

in the ...\commands\defaults subdirectory of the user settings.
Upper area
Confirm Exits the command. The dialogue closes.

the Info tab. Click the icon with the left mouse button. The

Default settings Options are reset to the default settings. This also takes
place after the software has been restarted.
Redo Restores the settings from the last command selection.
Preview Switches the results preview on / off.
Input area
The input area contains embedded sections such as the direction selection, whose layout
and operation are identical in different commands. Decimal places can be entered with
a comma or decimal point. A comma is converted automatically to a point. Unnecessary
decimal places are automatically removed. Mathematical calculations are possible during
value input.
Angles can be input as deg:min:sec or deg min sec (separated by spaces) and will then be
converted to the selected format.
Depending on the context, you can double-click with the left mouse button in the graphics
area to automatically jump within commands to the next input required for the command
sequence. The Continue command (highlighted in bold) in the context menu indicates this
option. If the Apply command is highlighted in bold, the entries will be applied and the
calculation will be performed.
Buttons are marked with different colours in order to indicate the input status:
No selection has been made yet.
Selection is sufficient to execute the command.
No further selection can be processed. The command can be executed.
A further selection must be made.
Too much information in the selection. The software waits for the input to
be corrected.
The different colours used for the button labels mean:
• Neutral: Input is possible.
• Blue: Input is possible or necessary. Option selected.
• Grey: No input is possible.
You can use the TAB key to confirm an entry and move between the input fields.
Right-click the respective button and call the command.

Context menu
Different context menus with a selection of commands, such as entering text and values,
are offered in the different areas. Right-click to open the context menu.
Reset: Undo a selection.
Save selection: A selection of curves and a selection of faces can be saved separately. A
combination of curves and faces is not possible.
Add saved selection: Add a saved selection for curves or faces to a current selection.
The following are available for parameterisable inputs:
Variables: Select a variable from the list that is located in theEdit → Spreadsheet command.
A value assigned by a variable is marked with the icon .
Functions: Select one of the available mathematical functions from the list.
Constants: Select one of the available mathematical functions from the list.
Mathematical calculations
Mathematical calculations are possible during value input. A calculation may be preceded
by an equals sign, but does not have to be.
Table 3. Mathematical operations
a * b a times b
a / b a divided by b
a % b Find remainder of division of a divided by b
a + b a plus b
a - b a minus b
a & b bitwise a AND b
a | b bitwise a OR b
a ^ b bitwise a XOR b
If a or b is a string instead of a number, then the operation a + b will be performed as a
concatenation of strings and not as an arithmetic addition.
Table 4. Bit operations
a << b Shift a b bits to the left (fill with zeros)
a >> b Shift a b bits to the right (copy the sign)
a >>> b Shift a b bits to the right (fill with zeros)
Table 5. Mathematical functions:
abs(a) Absolute value of a
acos(a) Arc cosine of a
hyperCAD®-S
11

-- 25 of 600 --

asin(a) Arc sine of a
atan(a) Arc tangent of a
atan2(a,b) Arc tangent of a divided by b
ceil(a) Integer closest to a and not less than a
cos(a) Cosine of a
exp(a) e to the power of a
floor(a) Integer closest to a and not greater than a
log(a) Logarithm to base e
max(a,b) Maximum of a and b
min(a,b) Minimum of a and b
pow(a,b) a to the power of b
random() Pseudo-random number between 0 and 1
round(a) Integer closest to a
sin(a) Sine of a
sqrt(a) Square root of a
tan(a) Tangent of a
A function may be preceded by ‘Math.’, but does not have to be. The arguments of the
function must be separated by a comma and the decimal places must be separated by a
decimal point.

Bookmarks
Save the view in the graphics area with the view direction, zoom state, set render proper-
ties, visibility and workplane settings.
Views imported from third-party CAD software (for example, ‘Reference sets’ and ‘Com-
bined views’) are rendered as bookmarks.
Select the following commands on the context menu. To do this, press the right mouse
button on the tab.
: No image generated for the bookmark yet.
Populate: Generate an image as no image is available for the bookmark after importing the
CAD data.
Create new: Save the view in the graphics area as a bookmark. A name is automatically
generated for the bookmark. Use the Rename command to change the name of the book-
mark.
Apply: Display the view in the graphics area again as it is saved in the bookmark. To do
so, double-click the required bookmark with the left mouse button. Optionally select the
required bookmark in advance and call the command.
Delete: Delete the bookmark.
Recapture: Save a view other than the existing one in the bookmark. To do so, select the
bookmark.
Rename: Change the name of the bookmark.
Select several bookmarks by drawing a selection frame with the mouse. To do so, press the
left mouse button and drag the mouse.
Search for text inside this structure from the line at the upper edge of the tree structure.
Enter your search text. Matching results will be shown as the text is entered. Confirm your
input with the ENTER key. The search history is then saved temporarily. In order to show
multiple different content items at the same time, use space characters to separate text
fragments. The search command is not case sensitive.
Clear: Remove the search string again. To do this, right-click the line to display the context
menu: Select the command there.

Coordinates
Display the moving coordinates of the cursor on the current workplane or the coordinates of
a snap point in relation to the current workplane.
View coordinates are displayed while the view is being changed. The display automatically
adapts itself to the unit of measure selected under Options / properties. On the context
menu, you can select the values and copy them to the clipboard.
Workplane
The entry of constructive values such as coordinates relates to the current workplane. A
new document is created automatically with a world workplane. Workplanes can be saved
together with transformation information and selected directly as references for moving and
copying, for example.
Determine the current workplane and control the visibility of the axes of the workplanes
created in the document:
Activated The X, Y and Z axes are displayed at the coordinate origin of
the workplane.
Deactivated The X, Y and Z axes are not displayed at the coordinate origin
of the workplane.
New work-
plane
Newly created workplane: Commands for modifying the work-
plane directly affect the new workplane. When a name is en-
tered, the workplane is stored permanently in the document.
The current workplane is highlighted in bold. To change the current plane, select the entry
with the left mouse button, right-click and then select Set current on the context menu.
To rename a workplane, click the name of the workplane with the left mouse button, change
the text and then apply the new name by pressing the ENTER key or by clicking with the left
mouse button outside the text input area.
To delete a workplane, use the Delete command on the context menu. Select the work-
plane with the left mouse button and right-click to open the context menu. You cannot
delete the current workplane.
If a workplane cannot be deleted, check the following possible causes:
• The workplane is being used, for example, as a reference system in the print box or for
dimensions.
hyperCAD®-S
17

-- 31 of 600 --

• The workplane is being used in an Undo / Redo action. Release the workplane by saving
and opening the document.
Active, currently set workplane (displayed in the graphics area)
Inactive, visible workplane (displayed in graphics area)
Associative: The workplane is linked with the entities selected for creation. Modifications to
the entities are carried over to the linked workplane.
The workplane is marked with an associative lock symbol. The size of the asso-
ciative lock symbol changes relative to the workplane display size.
The workplane is automatically assigned with a name. The automatically gener-
ated workplane name also references the ID of the linked entity.
An associative workplane is marked with a lock symbol in the WP tab.
A tooltip lists the linked entities. Rest the mouse pointer on the symbol to show the linked
entities. For modifications, select the appropriate workplane in the WP workplane tab and
run the command.
The translation and rotation are displayed for each workplane. Rotations are listed in the
order Z, Y, X. This corresponds to the transformation sequence. A workplane that has been
selected is highlighted as selected in the graphics area. The current workplane may be
hidden!
The world workplane can be enabled or disabled when creating a new document. Under
File → Options → Options / properties → Model → Workplane, select the Show with new
document option.

Related topics
Model > Workplane (page 61)

Model
List all entities of the loaded document.
Each entity can be selected directly for use in commands. An entity that has been selected
is marked as selected in the graphics window. You can select multiple entries by pressing
the CTRL or SHIFT key while clicking with the left mouse button. This also applies within
groups and shapes.
Different entity types such as faces and curves are differentiated more precisely, for ex-
ample, as circles, ellipses, extrusions, cylinders or plane faces and are displayed with a
corresponding icon.
The hole entity type can be specified more precisely using the thread processing property.
Sort by Entity type and ID of the entities by clicking the column header of the required
column with the left mouse button. Click again to reverse the sorting direction.
Show and hide entities directly on the context menu. The status is displayed:
Entity is hidden.
Entities within a group or a solid are hidden.
Grouping
Collect entities into a group.
Use the Edit → Group / ungroup command to create a new group, assign entities to an
existing group or move them to another group. Separate entities from a group. A group may
contain other groups.
Entities that originate from a group entity, such as lines from the boundary of a face, are
always inserted into the highest hierarchical level of the model structure. If an entity within
a group is modified, for example, a face is modified using Modify → Untrim face, the entity
remains a member of the group.
A new group is created on the active layer.
In groups, the display of entities is also influenced by whether the group is shown or hidden.
Entities within a hidden group will not be displayed, regardless of whether the entities
themselves are shown or hidden.
Selected entities within a group can be saved in a separate file.

Selection
The selection filter controls which entities should be included in or excluded from a selec-
tion, for example, using Window. The entities are therefore filtered for the selection process.
Different filter options are available. You can filter by Entities, Attributes and possible combi-
nations of different attributes using Advanced. Entities allows you to include entity types in
the selection or exclude them. Attributes allows you to examine layers or colours across
different entities. To select according to further criteria, you can construct an extended
Advanced selection filter.
You adjust the size by dragging the edge with the left mouse button. NURBS combines all
NURBS geometries.
Deactivate the selection filter for curves and activate the selection filter for faces in the
Wireframe view to select a face.
Activating and deactivating
Entities and attributes can be activated and deactivated by clicking the icon in front of the
individual entry.

Inactive

mouse button or SHIFT key and left mouse button.
Double-click with the left mouse button to activate the respective entry and deactivate all
other entries.
Depending on whether an entry or subentry is selected, you can right-click to open a

Open and closed entities
Some entity types may be composed of other entity types. For example, a solid of the
‘shape solid’ type is made up of faces. It may be necessary to optionally select the entire
solid or just some of the faces of the solid.
Open and closed entities therefore exist. Clicking the button with the right mouse button
opens a menu in which the ‘Entity open’ or ‘Entity closed’ option can be set for group,
shape and feature entities.
Set the ‘Entity open’ or ‘Entity closed’ option for V-sketch entities.
The set combination is displayed with the following icons:
Entity open - allows you to select entities that are enclosed in another entity. To
do this, also activate the required entity type of the enclosed entity.
Entity closed - you can only select the complete entity.
The settings are displayed as a button with changing icons:
‘Feature closed’ - you can only select a complete feature.
hyperCAD®-S
21

-- 35 of 600 --

‘Group closed’ - you can only select complete entities within a group.
‘Solid closed’ - you can only select a complete solid.
‘Group closed’ and ‘Feature closed’ are combined, solids are open.
‘Solid closed’ and ‘Feature closed’ are combined, groups are open.
‘Solid closed’ and ‘Group closed’ are combined, features are open.
A combination has been set - use ‘All’ to open or close everything.
The setting cannot be changed.
Attributes – filtering by layer and colour
You can filter by layer and colour attributes in order to make a selection. The layer and the
colour are defined by formulating a condition or by incorporating the attributes of an entity:
You formulate conditions in the condition list. A condition comprises a property,
an operator and a value or value range.
Use + to add a further condition and - to remove a condition. When you use the OR opera-
tor, multiple conditions act independently of each other. If you want multiple conditions to
apply, they must be linked using the AND operator (for advanced attributes only).
Transfer the attributes (colour, layer) of the entities directly from the graphics
area. Select the command and click the entity with the left mouse button.
Advanced:
To select according to further criteria, an advanced selection filter can be set up. These
criteria include system information and the geometric attributes of entities or their represen-
tation, among other things. For example, you can filter for all red cylinder faces with a
diameter between 9.5 and 10 mm.
To do this, formulate the conditions in the condition list.
Then proceed as usual with the entity selection options, for example, make a selection
using Select window. Entities will then be included in or excluded from the selection in
hyperCAD®-S
22

-- 36 of 600 --

accordance with the selected conditions. After the selection had been made, the conditions
are automatically discarded.
A condition list is active.
No conditions are active.
Reset the selection filter to the previous setting. The command in the upper
toolbar is active if the selection filter has been changed.
Visibility
The visibility filter controls the visibility of entities in the graphics area using different select-
able attributes.
The tab is divided into the Entities, Layer, Colour, Tags, User defined tags and Stored filters
sections. The size of each section can be adjusted by dragging the edge of the section with
the left mouse button. The layout is saved automatically. You can rearrange columns (drag
the table header with the left mouse button) and sort columns (click the table header with
the left mouse button).
The sections are operated similarly. Each section of the visibility filter represents a part of
the whole. If all the properties in a section are deactivated, nothing is visible.
Click the icon with the left mouse button to control the action of the condition, that is, the
visibility of the entities.
Property activated.
Property deactivated.
Some attributes of the branch are activated and others are not; activation also
affects all child attributes.
‘Entity open’ allows you to control the visibility of enclosed entities, such as the
visibility of faces within a shape. Make sure to also activate the required entity
type.
‘Entity closed’ - an entity such as a shape can only be displayed or hidden in its
entirety.
The ‘Entity open’ and ‘Entity closed’ options affect visibility in the Layer section. The ‘Entity
open’ option is always worked with automatically in the Colour section.
The number of combined entities (such as shape solids) is displayed in round brackets,
irrespective of the visibility, even if ‘Entity open’ has been selected.
The visibility filter has priority over the Hide and Unhide commands. It is applied immediate-
ly to new entities. A warning is output on the Info tab. Filter settings also apply to hidden
entities.

Tags
To filter the display of entities, you can capture and combine graphic and geometric attrib-
utes plus system properties using conditions.
Define condition
You formulate conditions in the condition list. A condition comprises a property, an operator
and a value or value range.
Use + to add a further condition and - to remove a condition. When you use the OR opera-
tor, multiple conditions act independently of each other. If you want multiple conditions to
apply jointly, you must link them using the AND operator.
In addition, an (Complementary) condition is inserted automatically. This applies to all
entities outside of the defined conditions.
Applying an OR operator
All entities to which an activated condition applies are visible. If all the conditions are
activated, all the entities will be visible. If all the conditions are deactivated, all the elements
will be invisible.
This behaviour is similar to the functionality in the Entities, Layer and Colour sections,
where conditions are implicitly linked with an OR operator.
Applying an AND operator
In AND operations, the conditions are connected with each other and act together. If a
condition is activated or deactivated, all the other conditions are also changed accordingly.
Table 14. Commands in the Tags visibility filter
Command Explanation
All active Activates all the conditions.
All inactive Deactivates all the conditions.
Activate only this Activates the selected condition.
Activate all except this Activates all conditions apart from the selected con-
dition.
Toggle Activates all inactive conditions and deactivates all
active conditions.
Suspend You can use the Suspend command to temporarily
deactivate a condition in a group of AND conditions.
This means that you do not need to delete a condi-
tion when it is not needed for a while.
Activate Reactivates an inoperative condition in a group of
AND conditions.
Delete Deletes the selected condition.
The commands are selected using the context menu (right-click to open) or directly via an
icon.
hyperCAD®-S

User-defined tags
To filter attributes, conditions for tags defined by users can be combined. Operation is the
same as with tags.
Stored filters
You can store combinations of activated and deactivated filters that you frequently require.
Right-click the Stored filters node and select the Save current filter command. An entry
is created. Click to modify the name. To reuse the stored filter configuration, right-click to
select the respective entry and then select the Apply command. Use the Delete command
to remove the stored selection filter from the list.

Tags
Tags allow you to manage attributes and information that can be linked to entities. User-de-
fined tags can be created. Categories, Keyword tags and Quantified tags are available for
the purpose of input and organisation.
Procedure:
1. Create one or more tag categories (such as ‘Tool’).
2. Create keyword tags or quantified tags in the tag categories (for example, ‘Nozzle
side’).
3. Select the entities and assign tags to the entities.
4. Apply the tags. For example, tags can be used on the Visibility tab to control the
visibility of complex workpieces.
hyperCAD®-S
29

contain keyword tags, quantified tags and further tag categories.

category, Create keyword tag and Create quantified tag. Select the required command.

Mouse operation
The following commands are assigned to different buttons and scroll wheel of the mouse:
Left mouse button:
• Entity selection, for example.
• Holding down the SHIFT key at the same time allows you to add further entities to a
selection.
• Holding down the CTRL key at the same time allows you to add an additional entity to a
selection.
• Holding down the CTRL key at the same time allows you to remove an entity from a
selection. This action automatically considers all identical curves, boundaries and edges
for the click position, even if an entity lying below was previously selected.
• It is possible to change the name of an entry after a simple (long) click on the entry, for
example, a layer name or the name of a workplane.
• Double clicking activates an entry, for example, updates a layer, activates an entity type
to filter during selection or only makes the required entity type visible.
Right mouse button:
• Clicking a button in the command dialogue opens a menu with the ‘Reset’ command that
allows you to discard a selection.
• Right-click to open a context menu that displays a selection of commands. The com-
mands that are available depend on the position of the mouse pointer.
• If the mouse pointer is positioned on a tab edge and an icon, right-click to display the tab
and toolbar selection.
Scroll wheel:
• The view in the graphics area changes depending on which command and which buttons
are active.
• If the mouse is positioned on the tab headers of a tab, you can scroll through the tabs.
The view stops changing if an entity is clicked using a 3D input device during rotation. The
mouse click is handled preferentially. Dynamic highlighting with a 3D input device during
rotation is also possible if you move the mouse slightly.
Keyboard shortcuts
You can also use keyboard shortcuts to call commands. The key combinations are dis-
played in the menu bar after the command names and they can be modified using the
Customise shortcuts command.
The following options are also available:
• F11 Display borderless program window.
• ALT+I ‘Invert selection’.
• ESC Reset selection filter to default setting.
• CTRL+F Open the Search and start command tab and set the input focus on the tab.
Different tabs can be opened directly using keyboard shortcuts. The TAB key can be used
to switch between input fields in a dialogue.
Some keyboard shortcuts always work, even if the current input focus is on a window,
dialogue or a tab (for example, in a chain selection).
Press the ENTER key to recall the previous command.

The software is operated on a screen with touch screen function with multi-touch finger
gestures.

Toolbars

graphics area. Custom toolbars are highlighted in bold. Enter a name. Go to “Commands”
and drag the desired commands to the new toolbar. Drag the toolbar itself by the handle to
the desired position.

Toolbars
Use Delete to delete custom toolbars. Default toolbars cannot be deleted or renamed.
Reset: Restore the original condition. A modified tab is marked by (*).
The toolbars can be positioned horizontally or vertically at any edge in several rows or
columns. To do this, click the handle of a toolbar with the left mouse button, move the bar
and release it in the required position. If a bar is minimised, click the double arrow icon with
the left mouse button to display all the commands of the bar.
Toggle the toolbars here, in the menu bar or in the title bar of a tab on and off. Right-click
on the menu bar or title bar of a tab to display a context menu with all of the available
toolbars. All visible toolbars are marked. Clicking on an entry with the left mouse button
switches the display of a toolbar on or off.
Vary the size of the icons continuously between 16 x 16 pixels and 96 x 96 pixels. Position
the cursor on the bar and, while holding down the CTRL key, set the required size with the
scroll wheel.
In user-designed toolbars, icons for new commands need to be updated manually. Other-
wise, update the toolbar by clicking Reset toolbar.

Keyboard shortcuts
Change keyboard shortcuts.
File → Options → Keyboard shortcuts
You can use keyboard shortcuts to call commands. The key combinations are displayed in
the menu bar after the command names and can be modified.
Some options can also be set using a keyboard shortcut. These include:
• Toggle ‘Dynamic highlight’ on / off.
• Toggle ‘Self shadows’ on / off.
• Activate or deactivate ‘Anti-aliasing’.
• Save an image of the current view with a white background in JPEG format in the
document directory.
• Save document and image of the current view in JPEG format. This is not possible for
read-only documents.
Select a command in the structure tree and double-click in the row with the left mouse but-
ton. Press a key combination on the keyboard. This combination is entered in the dialogue.
If the key combination is already in use, select another combination. Use the button to the
right of the line to delete a key combination that is no longer required. In the lower part
of the dialogue, select the area of the application in which the keyboard combination is
to work, for example, just in the graphics area, only on the tabs or throughout the entire
application. Avoid using single letters and the key combination ALT+[letter] – otherwise the
input in certain text fields will override these key combinations and no longer be possible.
Combinations such as ALT+SHIFT+[letter] or ALT+CTRL+[letter] can be used instead. Op-
erating system key combinations such as CTRL+C (for copying to the clipboard) are fixed
and cannot be changed.
Search for text inside this structure from the line at the upper edge of the tree structure.
Enter your search text. Matching results will be shown as the text is entered. Confirm your
input with the ENTER key. The search history is then saved temporarily. In order to show
multiple different content items at the same time, use space characters to separate text
fragments. The search command is not case sensitive.
Clear: Remove the search string again. To do this, right-click the line to display the context
menu: Select the command there.

Colour and display
Entities are displayed in the graphics area based on assigned colour attributes and light
settings.
Faces within a solid can have different colours. If the Visibility of a colour is deactivated,
only the edges are still displayed for all views with edges. A solid is not displayed at all if the
visibility for all colours within a solid is deactivated.

Light settings
The lighting in the graphics area is controlled using an ambient light source and four
directional light sources. The way in which an entity is displayed is not determined simply by
these light sources; the colour of the displayed entity also affects how it is displayed.
The ambient light provides basic background brightness. Light 1 to Light 4 are light sources
with directed, parallel rays of light that each illuminate the model from the direction of a
direction vector 1 	. This direction vector is entered as a unit vector (X, Y, Z) between -1.0
and +1.0. Directional light has no origin point. The direction of the light can remain constant
from the perspective of the viewer despite view changes, or it can move with the model.
(-1,-1,+1)

Edit colour library
Change colour attributes.
Edit → Edit colour library
Change colour attributes.
Colour library
Name shows the name of the active colour library. Description displays an optionally saved
explanation. In the colour selection section, select the colour to be changed.
Use Set default to apply changes to the colour library so that they are not only available in
the current document but also in other new documents with this colour library. The settings
are stored in the default.hcmaterials.xml file. Export writes the settings to a freely chosen
*.hcmaterials.xml colour library file.
Edit colour
Select a colour to change in the colour overview.
Diffusive: Diffuse reflection adds a spatial, three-dimensional impression to the display with
the help of the colour that has been selected. An imaginary, infinitely distant light source
with parallel rays of light is modelled. Click the left mouse button to open a dialogue in
which you can select a colour.
Ambient: Select a colour for the reflection of the ambient lighting that can be used to
give the colour an even and flat, two-dimensional appearance. This lighting provides basic
background brightness. Click the left mouse button to open a dialogue in which you can
select a colour.
Specular: In specular reflection, bright spots are created by means of a headlamp light
source in order to depict smooth surfaces. Click the left mouse button to open a dialogue in
which you can select a colour.
Transparency: You can set the transparency of the colour and thus the respective entity to
between 0% (non-transparent) and 100% (not visible).

Set the file format and select a document. Specific options can be defined depending on the

All (*.*) and All supported formats are used to display all file formats or all supported file
formats. In both cases, no interface format options can be selected.
As is usual in WINDOWS, a visual preview of the file contents is provided in the Windows
File Explorer. Set ‘Large Icons’ for this, for example.
In Windows File Explorer, system information can be determined for
hyperCAD®-S *.hmc
documents and *.hmct templates. As such, prior to opening a document, ensure that the
correct software version can be run.
• The Windows tool tip displays the
hyperCAD®-S version and the
hyperMILL® version.
• The file properties in Windows show an additional ‘OPEN MIND Info’ tab, which includes
all the available information. The tab is only shown if a *.hmc document or a *.hmct
template is selected. If several files are selected, the tab is not shown in order to avoid
any confusion.
If the document is password protected, it may not be possible to retrieve some information,
for example the version.
The prerequisite is that the dynamic program library thumbnailcom.dll is registered in Win-
dows. The registration is performed automatically during installation using the standard
hyperMILL® installation kit.
If a document is opened more than once, the second and all further instances of the
document will be read-only. Even if the first instance is closed, it cannot be overwritten by
the second or further instances of the document.
Warning for file opening!
A report is opened for defective entities automatically if there is a need to remove these
entities. To this end, the ID and type of the deleted entities and the errors found are output.
IMPORTANT
Carefully check the imported model!

Merge
Open files with different file formats and merge the data into the current docu-
ment.
File → Merge
In the first step, set the file format and select a document. Options for controlling the import
process can be defined depending on the format.
All (*.*) and All supported formats are used to display all file formats or all supported file
formats. In both cases, no interface format options can be selected.
Take over information
In the second step, specify which information is to remain in the existing document. Option-
ally, Hidden entities, Workplanes and Unused tags, that is, previously unused user-defined
tags, can be loaded or discarded. To transfer mating pairs, select the Mating option.
Variables: Import variables into the document. Where variable names have already been
assigned, an underscore and digit sequence will be added to the imported variables auto-
matically.
Metadatas: Import metadata as user-defined tags. Values in existing tags are overwritten. A
warning is output for this. To avoid overwriting, the data to be imported can be alternatively
imported as a group to which the metadata is assigned.
The file path of the document to be loaded is displayed and you can click Copy in the
context menu to copy the path to the clipboard.
Group entities
Optionally combine entities into a group when transferring.
Group name: Enter a name for the new group.
Positioning mode
On world: Place the entities of the document to be loaded with its world coordinates in the
current document on the world workplane. The current workplane in the current document is
not taken into account.
On WP: Place the entities of the document to be loaded with its world coordinates in the
current document on the current workplane. The world workplane in the current document is
not taken into account.
WP on WP: Place the entities of the document to be loaded on the current workplane in the
current document. The world workplane in the current document is not taken into account.

Conflicts that arise during an import are solved as follows for Workplanes, Layers, Colours
and user-defined tags Tags:
Do not load: Information that is currently being used is not loaded.

-- 64 of 600 --

Add tag: Label inserted entities with a tag. Enter a tag name.
Precision
Tolerance: Two entities to be compared are considered to be different if the check positions
calculated by the software differ so much that they are no longer located within a still
permissible distance from each other. Enter a value for this maximum permissible deviation.
Skip small entities: During the import, discard faces that have an area that is equal to or
less than the value specified. Whereby the tolerance specified here is squared: tolerance²
e.g. (0.01 mm)² = 0.0001 mm².
Distance between NURBS entities
The comparison of NURBS faces can take place based on the actual existing tessellation
of the faces or based on a new (more precise) tessellation to be calculated over all NURBS
faces. In this way, consolidate the check for NURBS faces.
Tessellation tolerance: Enter a tessellation tolerance for a finer tessellation of the NURBS
faces.
Simplify
Use simplified rotational faces: For conical hole ends and, for example, hole cylinders con-
sisting of multiple entities, the entities are first simplified to one entity and then compared.
Ensure that geometrically identical data are not recognised as different. Switching the
option on and off changes the comparison result.
Types
Curves: Compare curves.
Edges: Compare solid edges.
Polylines: Compare polylines.
Faces: Compare faces.
Meshes: Compare meshes.
PMI: Compare Product and Manufacturing Information (PMI).
Points: Compare points.

hyperCAD®-S
52

-- 66 of 600 --

Protect a document by entering a user-defined password. Enter a password. Show the input
in clear text using Show password. Save the document. A distinction is made between
upper and lower case.
The document will only open now after you have entered the correct password. A protec-
ted document can only be opened using the File → Open command in the file selection
dialogue. A neutral lock icon is shown instead of a thumbnail in the file selection dialogue.
You cannot open it by double-clicking or using a drag-and-drop operation.
Thumbnail for a protected file. The icon can only be displayed if there is a connection
between the file format and software in Windows Explorer.
Once a password has been assigned, the document continues to be protected even when it
is saved in the future.
To delete a password, call Protect again. Remove the password in the dialogue. If the
document is now saved, you will no longer be prompted when you open the document
again.
NOTE
Without the password, the document cannot be opened. If you lose the
password, the document will be unusable. No Undo is possible.
Close
Close current document and its program window.
File → Close
When you close the document, you will be asked if you want to save it. The following
options are available for selection:
Yes: Saves current document and closes it. If the document has not been saved yet, you
will be asked if you want to save it.
No: Closes current document. Any changes that you have made since the last save will be
discarded.
Cancel: Document is not saved and remains open.

Exit the current application.

If you have not saved the current version of the document, the software will prompt you to
save the data before exiting.

PLM documents
PLM-supported document management with the
hy-
perMILL® PLM Connector.
PLM Connector → Browser
Teamcenter Connector
Windchill Connector
The
hyperMILL® PLM Connector is a software application that provides an interface be-
tween a PLM system and OPEN MIND software. This software makes it possible to search
for documents directly in the PLM system. CAD documents can be downloaded to the local
computer to carry out NC programming with
hyperMILL®. All
hyperMILL® project data is
automatically combined, added to the PLM system and versioned there. The complexity of
a PLM system is reduced to an extent appropriate for NC programming. The
hyperMILL®
PLM Connector can be adjusted to a large extent to the requirements of a company and the
individual configuration of a PLM system. This software documentation therefore describes
a typical implementation as an example.
The PLM system itself must be adapted in order to be able to create a data object that can
contain
hyperMILL® data. A software installer is provided for this purpose.
The
hyperMILL® PLM Connector supports the PTC Windchill PLM system. Support for
other PLM systems is available on request.
NOTE
Data must be organised into projects before it can be automatically
combined. For this reason,
hyperMILL® PLM Connector automatically
enables the Projekt option in Document → Directories in the hyperMILL →
Setup → Settings command.

• Start work preparation for a new project:

Search
Search for documents
Search for documents stored in the remote PLM system. The search works roughly like in
the PLM system itself, and offers selected commands necessary to work in
hyperMILL®.
1. Enter a search string.
The search string must consist of at least three characters. This constrains the scope
of the search in advance.
2. Select one or more of the following document types Types for display, without which
the search cannot begin:
• hyperMILL Docs: Shows all documents in the PLM system created by
hy-
perMILL®.
• CAD Docs: Shows original CAD data in a range of formats, from which *.hmc
projects can be generated.
In the PTC Windchill PLM system, it is also possible to show the PTC Windchill *.wtpart
data records for analysis purposes that are used to manage all versions, components,
assemblies, and so on, of a project. They cannot be checked in or out for local editing..
The *.hmp archive file format contains all
hyperMILL® project data for management in
the PLM system.
3. Refine searches using search criteria in Filters:
All Show all versions of searched documents.
hyperCAD®-S
55

-- 69 of 600 --

REL only Only show versions of searched documents in the ‘Released’ status.
Latest Only show the last version of the searched documents.
4. Use Search to start the search process.
The found documents appear in a table with their respective document properties. The
table shows selected document properties from the PLM system. The selection can be
adjusted if necessary.
Using the search result
1. To use a CAD document or a
hyperMILL® document, click the table row with the
required search result to highlight it.
2. Then select an action Actions:
• Create new HMC: Downloads a CAD data record. The data1 is converted, gen-
erating an *.hmc document that is saved in the directory selected under Settings →
Settings → Local Folders → Working. The original downloaded CAD data record is
then automatically deleted on the local computer.
• Save as: In the current
hyperMILL® document, replace the associated CAD data
record with another CAD data record. In other words, thereby reuse existing job list
structures for another CAD data record, for example.
• Add to HMC: Downloads a CAD data record. The data format1 is converted and
inserted into a document already opened locally. This inserts, for example, the CAD
data for a fixture into the project. Metadata from the data record being inserted is
discarded. All entities are placed on an additional layer. The number of the part is
used as the layer name.
• Open in hyperMILL: Downloads an *.hmc file and opens it directly in
hyperMILL®
to start or continue NC programming.
Clear results discards the displayed search results.
Document
Shows information on the currently open local document.
• hyperMILL Document: Name, Version, Status, State, Modified by, Last Modification as
information on the
hyperMILL® document in the PLM system. This information is only
generated after initial check-in in the PLM system. The selection of metadata can be
adjusted if necessary.
The version of the
hyperMILL® document on the server is more recent than
the local version.
If a document has been checked out by someone else, this will be pointed out.
A document that only exists locally is edited.
Working with the latest version and checked-in status.

• at version: If a warning indicates that the local version is out of date, download the
latest version.
• at version: Create a next revision, e.g. B. A.3 becomes B.1 or 00.3 becomes 01.1.
• at State: Change the status of a document, e.g. when NC programming is finished
and the status should be changed from "In Work" to "Released". Normally documents
in the "Released" state cannot be checked out. (It depends on the guidelines that are
set for the PLM.)
• : for Location: Specify a destination on the server for saving the data. To do this,
first select the topmost structural unit in the settings in Context selection.
• Reference CAD Part: File name, Version, Status, State of the original CAD data (for
example, from design) in the PLM system at the time the *.hmc file was generated. The
selection of metadata can be adjusted if necessary.

version.

Working with the latest version of the CAD data and checked-in status.
• for Version: If a warning indicates that the local version is outdated, download the
latest version into the current document.
Select an action in Actions for the currently open local document:
• Upload: Add a current state of work as a personal ‘backup copy’ not visible to others
in the PLM system. The document has the ‘Checked-out’ status. No further iterations of
the document are generated in the PLM system. Newer uploaded files overwrite older
files.
• Check in: Uploads and checks in the locally opened project. All associated
hy-
perMILL® data is collected, compressed and also uploaded. Successful check-in is con-
firmed by the message ‘Check in complete’. The data is inserted into the
hyperMILL DOC
as
Primary content.
• Check out: Check out the locally opened document again. This protects the data
from being modified by others.
• Undo Check out: Reverses check out, discards all local changes.
• , Check Status: Checks whether the locally available project data still has the
same version as the checked-in data.
• Save as: In the current
hyperMILL® document, replace the associated CAD data record
with another CAD data record. In other words, thereby reuse existing job list structures for
another CAD data record, for example.
• Revise: Based on a data record in the ‘Released’ status saved in the PLM system,
Version A, creates a branch data record, Version B.1, for editing.
• Select PLM folder: Specify a destination on the server for saving the data. To do this, first
select the topmost structural unit in the settings in Context selection.

When starting the software, all the factory system settings are loaded first. After that, all the
local settings are applied. These settings are then updated with the company settings.
Start software Installation settings User settings Company settings
If an option has not been specified in the company settings, the previously loaded setting
is used. If company settings are used, all settings can still be changed locally, including
the original system settings that were loaded and the company settings. The settings are
effective from when a change is made and do not affect how existing entities are displayed.

Create *.hmc_bak file: When you save a document, a backup file of the previous version of

Auto backup at saving reminder: If changes are being made, save a copy of the
current state of work at regular scheduled intervals (as defined in Save remind-

folder. The %temp%\OPEN MIND\AutoBackup folder is offered by default.
Use previous CAD direct interfaces: This choice enables customers, who were users of
a
hyperCAD®-S CAD direct interface until 31 December 2019 (effective date) to continue
using the interface technology used to date by them.
Search for external markups when saving/loading: Enable or disable search for external
markups when saving and loading *.hmc documents. If the search is deactivated, markups
need to be manually imported. It is useful to disable the search if numerous files are located
in the same folder and it therefore results in long waiting times when opening a document.
The settings are stored in the applicationApplicationData.xml file.
User interface
Single document behaviour: Click Yes to use only one instance of the software. To open
a document, a document that is already open must be closed first. Click No to open each
document in a separate instance of the software.

displayed. To work with the language of the operating system, select default. If a language
is not available, English will be used for the user interface.

menu.

the number of columns.

er to save the document. Disable the command by entering 0.

Show with new document: The world workplane is enabled immediately in a new document.
Euler mode: Select the type of rotation. In the case of Fixed axes (extrinsic), rotation is
around the axes of the original workplane, which is assumed to be motionless. In the case
of Rotating axes (intrinsic), rotation is around the axes of the workplane itself, whereby their
orientation changes after each rotation.
Euler order: Select the transformation sequence for the rotation angle.

Length: Use the Unit option to select length units of millimetres or inches. Use the Millimetre
and Inch options to specify how many decimal places are displayed and whether units of
measure should be displayed in dialogues.
The units of measure are always output for volume, mass and density values.
Volume: Use the Unit of measure option to select a unit of measure. For each of the
available units of measure, select how many decimal places should be used when they are
displayed in dialogues. The cubic decimetre (dm³) unit of measure is preset for volumes.
Mass: Use the Unit of measure option to select a unit of measure. For each of the available
units of measure, select how many decimal places should be used when they are displayed
in dialogues. The kilogram (kg) unit of measure is preset for mass.
Mass density: Use the Unit of measure option to select a unit of measure. For each of the
available units of measure, select how many decimal places should be used when they are
displayed in dialogues. The kilogram on cubic decimetre (kg/dm³) unit of measure is preset
for the mass density.
The settings are stored in the documentModelProperties.xml file.

Degree symbol: Enter a symbol for degrees.
Minute symbol: Enter a symbol for minutes.

For various combinations of angle units, specify how many Decimal places should be
displayed and if the unit of measure should be displayed when outputting angles when Unit
of measure has been selected.

Highlight thickness: Select the line thickness for highlights.
Dynamic highlight: An entity or a boundary is highlighted in colour while it is touched by the

• Shaded dynamic highlight: Highlight the entire face instead of just the edge and UV lines
of an entity.

Graphic > System > Navigation
Selection radius: Size of the area in which an entity is selected if it has not been selected
precisely.
Endless rotation: The view continues to be rotated automatically after initiation until the user
stops the process. The acceleration of the mouse during initiation determines the speed of
rotation.
Dynamic render mode: Determines whether the existing render mode is changed during
rotation. Allows you to choose the render mode you require.
Dynamic rotation type: Cylindrical rotates the view around the axes of the screen. Spherical
rotates the view around the centre.
Rotation angular speed: Specifies the angle around which the view rotates when the mouse
is moved from left edge to right edge during rotation.
Implicit rotation target: View volume uses the middle of the model volume of the current
view as the rotation middle point. Ray cast looks for the centre of the program window and
uses this point as the rotation middle point. Automatic leaves the choice to the software.
Invert zoom: Changes the zoom direction for zooming with SHIFT + right mouse button.
Zooming into an image normally takes place when the mouse is moved downwards. If this
option has been selected, you zoom out when you move the mouse downwards.
Invert mouse wheel zoom: Invert the direction of the zoom when zooming with the mouse
wheel.
Mouse wheel zoom factor: Change the mouse wheel zoom speed. The value 1 is equivalent
to the settings from the operating system. Values between 0.1 and 1 make zooming slower.
Values between 1 and 10 accelerate zooming.
Keyboard pan step: Step size for panning with the keyboard.
Keyboard rotate step: Step size for rotating with the keyboard.
Keyboard zoom step: Step size for zooming with the keyboard. Select a value between 1
and 180 (default setting: 30).
Avoid highlighted entities overlap effects: Avoid visual effects on highlighted overlapping
entities. Warning, this process requires considerable processing power.
Smooth transition view: Select a smooth transition from one view to another view.
Smooth transition time (sec.): Enter the smooth transition time in seconds.
The settings are stored in the viewViewProperties.xml file.

sary to adapt the line thickness manually. After printing, the original line thicknesses are

Hidden line: Choose display of hidden lines or edges in the graphics area.
Render cache: One-off rendering of entities when a document is loaded. The displayed en-

Silhouettes: Activate the display of the silhouette of the model as a graphic in the current
view. With ‘Edge view’ and ‘Edge view with hidden lines’, model edges are added that are

TrueType text quality: Modify the tessellation precision of TrueType font text elements.
Zebra lines: Select the number of zebra lines for zebra analysis.

Clipping plane to all entities: Toolpath entities and tools are included in the clipping plane.

Pick through transparent colour: Make entities that are hidden from view selectable through

Face simplification: Collect resulting coplanar faces into an entity. As a result of a feature
command, faces, but not features, are inserted in a solid.

Extension factor: Limit the extension of faces to find a new trim edge. Increase the factor

the solid.
Iterate: If the standard method for calculating direct modelling is not satisfactory, other

quality of the data included in the calculation. If such multiple calculation is not desired,
these repeated attempts can be disabled with this option.

mouse button and simply scroll through the values using the scroll wheel.

the form ‘name = value’.

still automatically assigned.

Leader line arrow head / Arrow head
Use Type 1 	to select the graphical appearance of the dimension arrow head. Enter the
size 2 	. If you do not want the colour to be taken over from the active colour attribute,
select the Own colour 3 	option and specify a colour. The Position of the arrow heads for a
dimension can be located inside or outside 4 	. Use the Follow text option to align the arrow
to the position specified for the measure text 5 	.
2
1
3 4 5
Reference lines
Use the Visibility 1 	option to control whether the dimension reference line is displayed on
the left, the right, both sides or not at all. Use Extension 2 	to extend the line beyond the
dimension arrow head. Gap 3 	ensures that there is a space between the line and the edge
of the model.
1 3
2
Measure position
Use the Horizontal 1 	options to select the position of the measure text on the measure
line. The measure text can, for example, be centred or positioned outside on one of the
two sides. To determine the position with the cursor, select the Follow cursor option. Use
the Vertical 2 	option to specify whether the measure text should be located above or in the
middle of the measure line. Use the Orientation 3 	option to specify whether the measure
text is aligned parallel to the measure line or is always displayed horizontally. The Text -
line distance 4 	value provides a gap between the measure text and measure line.

Surface finish
Select surface finish attributes.
Symbol:
• Basic symbolBase
• Machining required
• Machining prohibited
Area:
• Basic symbol
• Face texture: Only the applicable faces should have a surface finish according to
further specifications.
• All surface around: All faces all around should have the same surface finish.
• Surface texture and all faces around: All faces all around should have the same
surface finish. Here, the surface finish is determined by further specifications.
Manufacturing method: Enter the final state of a desired manufacturing process, surface
treatment or coating, for example, smoothed.
Sampling length: Enter a value or a roughness parameter.
Cutoff: Enter a value for the roughness parameter.
Additional values: Enter further values for the surface finish.
Lay direction: Enter the direction of grooves that are formed during material removing
machining:
• None: Indefinite grooving
• Approximately parallel: Select lay direction parallel to the projection plane (front view).
• Approximately perpendicular: Select lay direction perpendicular to the projection plane
(front view).
• Angular in both directions: Lay direction crossed, select in two oblique directions.
• Multi directional: Select grooves in many directions.
• Approximately circular: Select grooves approximately centric to the middle point.
• Approximately radial: Select grooves approximately radial to the middle point.
hyperCAD®-S

• Particulate, non-directional, or protuberant: Select a non-grooved surface, non-direc-
tional or grooving.
Waviness: Enter a value for the waviness if a surface should be so uneven that deviations
from an ideal surface occur periodically at longer intervals than the roughness.
Max. value: Enter further values for the surface finish.
Mean or min. value: Enter further values for the surface finish.
Machining allowance: Enter a value for a machining allowance.
Mating options
Configure options for mating.
File → Options → Mating options
Configure options for mating.
Tolerance
Linear tolerance: The linear tolerance is used for distancing, for example, for the distance of
a plane that must be parallel to another plane. For the distance of a rotation axis that should
lie on the target axis for coaxial mating, or for the maximum distance during coincident
mating. Enter a value for the permissible deviation.
Angular tolerance: The angular tolerance is used for alignment, for example, for the paral-
lelism of normals when mating planar faces, parallelism of axes for coaxial mating or for
perpendicularity during perpendicular mating. Enter a value for the permissible deviation.

OPEN MIND Software
Import and save different OPEN MIND data.
Own file formats
Interface formats of OPEN MIND software products.
*.hmc Document format
*.hmup Format for saving markups.
*.hmct Format for saving a geometry as a template for a new document
*.hmcgeom Native format containing geometric and topological data in binary, uncom-
pressed form including information on solids and meshes.
In addition, basic graphic properties (colour, line type and thickness, layer
name) are also saved.
*.hcplain Uncompressed document format
*.bnd BND is a
hyperMILL® format.
hyperMILL® boundaries can be imported.
The entities are inserted in the model tree. A group can be optionally created
around the imported data. The data is given its own layer with the name of
the file or with a specially specified layer name.
Table 25. Options for opening a *.bnd file
Property Description
General Layer: Specify an own layer name.
Colour: Overwrite the current colour with RGB value during
import.
Group entities: Create a group around the imported data.
Unit of measure
WARNING
Data is imported with the “mm” unit of
measure.
The format is not “inch” compatible (for
technical reasons). It does not contain any
information on the unit of measure. When
importing, “mm” is always assumed to be
the unit of measure. If the opened model
is in inches, content is usually imported too
small by a factor of 25.4.

Further file formats
The following file formats can be imported into
hyperCAD®-S:
• *.e3 with job list and entity selection
• *.e2
• *.gkd
All attributes such as colour, layer and number of isoparametric curves are supported. The
edge IDs of solids are retained. The stored view is transferred for the direction, zoom sec-
tion and render mode. Component information with hierarchy and names can be preserved.
The prerequisite is that you have a working version of
hyperCAD® installed in parallel and a
licence.
Bookmarks are converted to Visibility filters wherever possible. All layer settings within the
bookmarks are taken over. None of the other settings in the bookmarks are taken over.
An *.e3 file must have been saved using a
hyperMILL® version of 2009.2 or higher and
an active
hyperMILL® session in order to ensure that all
hyperMILL® selections can be
reproduced for
hyperCAD®-S.

General interfaces
Image
Open or insert an image and save model data as an image.
hyperCAD®-S
89

-- 103 of 600 --

Insert an image (such as a company logo) as a bitmap into a template for a title block.
Insert an image for sketching into a document as a background.
An image is managed as an Image entity type. Use the following commands to modify an
image.
• Scale
• Move / copy
• Mirror
• Hide
• Unhide
• Delete
The image entity type cannot be snapped.
How can an image be opened?
Select the File → Open or File → Merge command. Then select the Image file file type.
The following graphics formats are supported:
• *.jpg: Independent JPEG Group - format
• *.bmp: Windows bitmap - format
• *.tif: Tagged Image File - format
• *.png: Portable Network Graphics - format
• *.rgb: Silicon Graphics native raster - format
How can model data be saved as an image?
Select the File → Save as command and then select the Image file file type. Specify the set-
tings for saving an image. Enter a name for the file. Select a directory. Define the graphics
format by appending a file extension to the name. If you do not add a file extension, the
image will be saved in *.jpg format.
The following graphics formats are supported:
• *.jpg: Independent JPEG Group - format
• *.bmp: Windows bitmap - format
• *.tif: Tagged Image File - format
• *.png: Portable Network Graphics - format
• *.rgb: Silicon Graphics native raster - format
• *.ps: Encapsulated PostScript - format
Table 31. Settings for saving an image
Property Explanation
View size The image will be as large as the current graphics area.
Width (pixel) and Height
(pixel)
If View size is not selected, create the image with the speci-
fied height and width.
White background Select white as the background colour of the image.
View all Include all entities in the image and not just the entities that
are displayed in the current view.

3MF file format
Import *.3mf file.
3D Manufacturing Format or 3MF is an open source file format standard. It is an XML-
based data format specifically for additive manufacturing and contains information about
materials, colours and other information that cannot be represented in STL format.
Table 33. Options for opening files in the 3MF file format
Property Description
File Automatic saving after opening: After the import process, automatically
save the document with the original file name.
Conver-
sion toler-
ance
Entity precision: Points within the specified tolerance are handled as a
single point. This is a purely mathematical issue and does not concern
“point” geometric entities.

• Same as input file: Save the log file with the file name of the model in the

the file after import.

ACIS file formats
Import *.sat and *.sab files.
The 3D ACIS Modeler (ACIS) is a kernel for geometric modelling. ACIS supports two types
of file formats, Standard ACIS Text (SAT) and Standard ACIS Binary (SAB). The two
formats store identical information.
Table 35. Options for opening files in ACIS formats
Property Description
Filter enti-
ties
Include entity types and information specifically during import.
Solid con-
version
mode
Break solids into faces: Solids are broken into faces and faces are transfer-
red. The original layer assignment is retained.
Collect solid faces into groups: The original layer assignment is retained.
Collect solid faces into layers: The original layer assignment is lost when
combining on layers.
Create solid: Solids are created again for solids that exist in the original
system. The original layer assignment is retained.
In ...\bin\converters\cad interfaces\hcntCR_HE.ini, the value sld_tol_sf can
be changed in order to use a different tolerance when closing a solid in
relation to the precision of the entity.
Entity con-
version
modes
Create 3D boundaries: Generate curve entities from face boundaries.
Convert all faces to NURBS: All analytical faces are converted to NURBS
geometry.
Start hyperMILL feature recognition after model import: Feature recognition
is performed automatically directly after importing the data.
Model
conver-
sion mode
Create model structure: Generate the model structure of the model again.
The result may differ from the original model structure.
File Automatic saving after opening: After the import process, automatically
save the document with the original file name.
Conver-
sion toler-
ance
Entity precision: Points within the specified tolerance are handled as a
single point. This is a purely mathematical issue and does not concern
“point” geometric entities.
Layer as-
signment
Starting layer for faces: Specify starting layer for combining on layers dur-
ing the Solid conversion of faces.
Continue layer count: When importing another model into the same docu-
ment, the layer count is continued and does not start again at the defined
starting layer.

AutoCAD file formats
Import or save document in the AutoCAD *.dxf, *.dwg file format.
2D geometries, mesh entities, layers, colours, line types, text and dimensions are imported
from the paper or model space as vectorial graphics.
3D-data is saved as mesh data and 2D data.
Various options are available to display the text imported from *.dxf format with the correct
font:
Access AutoCAD font files with user variable:
In order to correctly display *.shx fonts, the software needs to know where the AutoCAD
font files are located.
• The font files must be stored in a folder with user access rights.
• Enter the folder in a new Windows system variable or ACAD user variable.
This is the best approach for correctly representing content.
Convert font file to another existing font :
Another option is editing the file: ...\hyperCAD-S\<Version>\files\converters\dxfloadfonts-
mapping.xml.
The edited file can be stored in the folder ...\Users\<Benutzername>\AppData\Roam-
ing\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\<Version>\Converter in order to prevent it being overwritten
during an update.
This file can be used to convert any *.shx font file into an existing *.ttf font file.

3
Interpolating: Read in points and interpolate curves from
them.
Approximating: Read in points and approximate curves from
them.
If there are only a few points, interpolation makes sense
because the curves run exactly through the points 4 	.
If there are many points, approximation is useful if the da-
ta is subject to measurement errors and these should not
be reflected. The approximation tolerance controls the maxi-
mum deviation of the generated curves from the points. The
generated curve is smoothed 5 	.
4 	5

vidual point coordinates.

commented out.

closed in quotes ".
Points are numbered Select the Yes option if the points or their rows are num-
bered.
Entity precision Enter a value.
Significant digits Enter a value.

Tessellation mode Existing: Use the document’s currently set tessellation toler-

Default: Write the data to the file with a specific, default
tessellation tolerance set for it.
Connected: Combine the tessellation of the individual enti-
ties into a connected tessellation of the entire surface of the
model data and save it (for example, for advanced process-
ing in a 3D print or in a simulation).
This mode is another way of creating the mesh itself: the
node points are the same on all edges in order to create a
‘connection’ between the nearest faces. The arrangement of
the triangles is recalculated according to the desired proper-
ties.
Tessellation tolerance Enter the tessellation tolerance for the Default tessellation
mode.
Connected Tessellation -
tessellation tolerance
Write the data to the file in the Connected tessellation mode
with the specified tessellation tolerance.
Connected Tessellation -
max edge length
Enter the maximum permissible edge length of the triangles
for the Connected tessellation mode.
Connected Tessellation
- max boundary edge
length
Enter the maximum allowed edge length of the triangles
whose edges form part of an entity’s boundary for the Con-
nected tessellation mode.

Wavefront OBJ file format
Import *.obj files.
OBJ is an open file format for saving three-dimensional geometric shapes. The format is
supported by many 3D graphics programs and is therefore suitable for sharing 3D models
across programs and platforms. The OBJ format stores geometric properties of an object or
grouped objects, such as corners, texture coordinates, normals, faces and smoothings.

The PMI is displayed and inserted as editable entities (text, dimensions and vector graph-

Product and Manufacturing Information (PMI) option.
To display the PMI, the ‘MyriadCAD’ font is required as the standard font in Win-

in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\[Version]\bin\converters\cad interfaces\[Ver-

Importing, filtering and renaming metadata names
Transfer user-defined properties (metadata) of a document, from solids and faces from
external CAD software as tags. To do so, select the Model conversion mode → Create
model structure option. Metadata is assigned as tags to the document or entities.
NOTICE
Filtering takes place first before renaming. This means that you need to
work with the original names to filter out metadata.
Filtering
A separate filter can be applied to metadata names for each format. The filter is formula-
ted in metadatafilter.xml. An example file is provided in the .../files/converters/cad interfa-
ces/conf/all/ directory.
This XML file is structured as follows:
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<MetadataFilter version="1.1">
<Format type="cv4">
<ConditionSet logical="or">
<Condition operator="ISLIKE">
<Reference>*inner*</Reference>
</Condition>
</ConditionSet>
</Format>
</MetadataFilter>
Use <Format> to specify the CAD interface format (to be imported) for which the filter
should be used.
• type="cv4" for CATIA V4
• type="cv5" for CATIA V5
• type="cv6" for CATIA V6
• type="par" for Parasolid
• type="pro" for PTC Creo
• type="slw" for SOLIDWORKS
• type="ugx" for Siemens NX
• type="inv" for Autodesk® Inventor®
• type="igs" for IGES
• type="stp" for STEP
• type="jto" for JT-Open
• type="3dm" for Rhinoceros®
• type="sle" for Solid Edge
• type="prc" for PRC (Product Represen-
tation Compact)
Conditions can be combined in <ConditionSet> with the logical operators AND or OR.
Each condition <Condition> can use a suitable operator. Various operators are available:
hyperCAD®-S
112

-- 126 of 600 --

• IS: Search for an exact match.
• ISLIKE: Search for matches using wildcard characters. Here, * matches 0 or more instan-
ces of any character or string and ? matches any character.
• STARTSWITH: Search for an existing substring at the beginning.
• ENDSWITH: Search for an existing substring at the end.
• CONTAINS: Search for an existing substring.
• DOESNTCONTAIN: Search for a non-existing substring.
Formulate the search string in <Reference> .
Example 1. Filter out metadata for electrode
Using the Ele_* reference and the ISLIKE operator, transfer all metadata that begins with
the
Ele_... string. To do so, formulate the following XML lines:
<Format type="pro">
<ConditionSet logical="or">
<Condition operator="ISLIKE">
<Reference>Ele_*</Reference>
</Condition>
</ConditionSet>
</Format>

Renaming
Metadata names can be renamed to tag names for each format. The instruction is formu-
lated in metadatarename.xml. An example file is provided in the .../files/converters/cad
interfaces/conf/all/ directory.
This XML file is structured as follows:
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<MetadataRename version="1.1">
<Format type="pro">
<NamesMap>
<Pair original="Original_Name">New_Name</Pair>
</NamesMap>
</Format>
</MetadataRename>
Use <Format> to specify the CAD interface format (to be imported) for which the filter
should be used. The abbreviations that can be used for this are described in the Fil-
ter (page 112) section
Within <NamesMap> with <Pair> enter a list of all metadata to be renamed. In the attribute
original the original name and as a property of <Pair> insert the new name.
Example 2. Rename metadata for electrode
Insert the original name in the original attribute and enter the new name as a property of
<Pair>. To do so, formulate the following XML lines:
<NamesMap>
<Pair original="Elec_gap01">T_ELECTRODE_ROUGHNESS_V</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_gap02">T_ELECTRODE_ROUGHNESS_VV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_gap03">T_ELECTRODE_ROUGHNESS_VVV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_gap04">T_ELECTRODE_ROUGHNESS_VVVV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_num01">T_NUMBER_V</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_num02">T_NUMBER_VV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_num03">T_NUMBER_VVV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_num04">T_NUMBER_VVV</Pair>
<Pair original="Elec_mat">T_ELECTRODE_MATERIAL</Pair>
</NamesMap>

Modes
Influences the path of the chain.
Stop at cross: The chosen contour is continuously selected until the contour line branches
A 	.
Minimal angle: The chosen contour is continuously selected B 	. Selecting the contour at a

2
Shortest way: The chosen contour is continuously selected. At a bifurcation, the further
