Electrode project: Select a directory for the milling project to undertake milling programming
and to create the derived electrodes.
If no electrode project directory has been selected, the directory of the opened *.hmc
document will be used as the directory for the electrode project data.
If an electrode project directory has been entered, a subdirectory with the name of the
electrode is created for each derived electrode.
The derived electrode is stored in this directory. A check is carried out to make sure that
the directory and electrode names match, and that no directory is overwritten or created
multiple times. All
hyperMILL® data is stored in the directory of the relevant electrode and
only appears there once. Reports are saved in the root directory of the milling project. This
means that the directory can be used as a root for backup copies.

Create reference system
Create a positional reference between the workpiece and electro-
des.
Electrodes → Create reference system
Electrode
Create a workplane for the positional reference between the workpiece and electrodes. As
an aid, a bounding box with snap positions that is exactly large enough to fit all selected
workpiece entities inside the box will be displayed. Selecting a snap position in the centre of
each side of the snap area will suggest the adjusted Z axis orientation for this side.
The selected Entities are applied as a collision model for the collision check and collision
simulation in
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center and are necessary for the contour-aided
selection of the electrode shape when creating the electrode.
The workplane is automatically assigned with a name. Specify the composition of the name
in the Electrode options.
IMPORTANT
The position and direction that serve as a point of reference for a refer-
ence system may not be subsequently changed if it has already been
used to generate electrodes. The positions of an electrode in X, Y and Z
are not associatively linked to the reference system. More faces can be
added to the bounding box when the identical reference point is selected
to update the existing reference system by overwriting it. Changing this
reference point leads to incorrect erosion measures. Likewise, the elec-
trode workplane linked to the electrode must not be changed manually.
The selected geometry is automatically locked by this command. Unintentional shifting or
scaling of the workpiece is prevented. The lock can be released manually at any time at
one’s own risk using the command Edit → Unlock entities. If manually unlocked, the lock
will no longer be set automatically afterwards. Only as long as the lock is present can the
software inform that the workpiece is being used for eroding and should not be changed.
NOTE
It is advisable to lock the workpiece again after making a change. To lock
the part again, reassign the eroding reference to all faces and select the
existing name.
Example 18. Add faces to expand the collision model
Faces can be added to a collision model by adding TAG information.
1. Determine the value of a face’s TAG that already belongs to the desired collision
model. To do this, select this face (not a whole solid!) and open the command TAGs →
Edit info. There, search for the TAG T_ELECTRODE_PART_FOR_EDM_* in the TAG
name column. Where * is a variable part of the TAG name that was assigned when the
collision model was created. Determine the value in the Value column and note it.
2. In the tab sheet TAGs in the tree structure, find the TAG TAGs → T_ELEC-
TRODE_CATEGORY → T_ELECTRODE_PART_FOR_EDM_* again. Right-click to
hyperCAD®-S
442

-- 456 of 600 --

open the context menu and select the Assign command. Within this command, select
the faces to be added to the collision model. And enter the noted value in the line
T_ELECTRODE_PART_FOR_EDM_*! Apply the entries.

electrode shape when creating the electrode.

Extract electrode geometry and add an electrode holder and other additional information
about an electrode.
A workplane in the electrode reference point is created in parallel. This is used for calcula-
tions while the electrode is being created, and is not bound to the electrode. A change to
this workplane has no effect on the electrode and its position coordinates. It can be used to
dimension the position reference.
An electrode cannot be changed retrospectively. To make changes, the electrode must be
created again. To change electrode holders and raw material, break up the group, delete
the electrode holder and the block and manually compose a new electrode using the user
defined command.
The software will automatically create a coordinate label for the electrode in relation to
the reference point. The coordinates and text can be read from every ‘View’ and in the
electrode summary.

Which additional information is provided when creating an electrode?
When creating an electrode, information is made available on the Info tab.
Info / warning Purpose
Length of useless raw ma-
terial
Distance from the electrode contact face to the start of
the raw material. Reduce this value by increasing the
block height. This reduces the remaining material to be
milled off.
Minimal rib distance In the case of multiple ribs, i.e., multiple electrodes on the
same block or electrode holder, the smallest distance be-
tween the ribs is given. Use this to check if the distance is
sufficient for the diameter of the planned milling tool.
A stabilisation extension is not included in the calculation.

Raw material dimensions The dimensions of the automatically selected raw materi-
al are displayed.

Virtual electrode
Create virtual electrodes from a master electrode
Electrode → Virtual electrode
Electrode
Create one or several virtual electrodes from an existing electrode, the ‘master electrode’.
This prevents identical electrodes from repeatedly appearing physically in the model, that
is, as a group. Instead, a master electrode is managed with reference to virtual electrodes.
This way, the same electrode can be programmed for erosion in different positions.
How is a virtual electrode created?
hyperCAD®-S
454

-- 468 of 600 --

1. Call the Move / copy A 	command. Select the master electrode 1 	. Make sure to select
the entire electrode. For this purpose, activate on the Selection tab the selection of an
entire group as an entity.
This will create copies of the master electrode, set up as a group in the model tree.
Note that this is not an electrode, but only a group containing all the information about
the master electrode.
NOTE
Only one copy per copy process can be created, regardless of the
number set under Copy 2
It is checked whether a copy collides with the workpiece geometry. A
warning is output on the Info tab.
All entities – the master electrode and its copies – are locked. To
unlock, the copies must be unlocked first. The master electrode can
be unlocked only after.
If a machine head is assigned to the master electrode, this machine
head and its C-axis are fully displayed as unrotated in the preview
during rotation. This only applies to this preview! During the collision
check, the C-axis geometry is rotated as desired and the rest of the
machine head geometry is regarded as fixed .
1
A
2
2. Then, call the Virtual electrode command B 	. Select a master electrode using Elec-
trode. Other entities cannot be selected. Select a copy from the table. Change its
status to Virtual II 	and select the reference system 4 	. The copy is “virtualised”, and its
colour changes. Information on this electrode is added to the master electrode. Upon
exiting the command, virtual electrodes are hidden, both in the graphics area and on
the Model tab 5 	.
hyperCAD®-S
455

-- 469 of 600 --

B I
3 	4
5
II
3. To unhide a virtual electrode and change its status, run the Virtual electrode command
and select the master electrode using the Electrode option. The virtual electrodes are
displayed again. The status and reference system can be changed.
II
III
With the Derive, Derive and milling and Export reports commands, all information about the
virtual electrodes is output.
The following changes are made when unlocking a master electrode:
hyperCAD®-S
456

-- 470 of 600 --

• Regarding the copy: The copies are unlocked, all tags related to this command are
removed, and the layers change back. The warning “The electrode is unlocked and not
more an virtual electrode!” is displayed.
• For the master electrode: the electrode is unlocked, and all tags related to this command
are removed. This includes tags with information on the virtual electrodes, which no
longer exist as a result. The warning “The electrode is unlocked and all virtual electrodes
are removed!” is displayed.
The following changes are made when unlocking a copy:
• Regarding the copy: the layers change back, and tags related to the command are
removed. The warning “The electrode is unlocked and not more an virtual electrode!” is
displayed.
• For the master electrode: Tags related to the command are removed. The electrode is
unlocked when there are no more copies or virtual electrodes that belong to it.
When mirroring, a normal electrode is created from a virtual electrode.

Electrode: Select a master electrode 3 	.

Reuse an electrode
Reuse an existing, fully constructed electrode “Electrode001” from a project A in a project
B.
Requirements
• The name of the EDM reference system must be identical in both projects, otherwise
there will be no collision check for the virtual electrodes created from the copy.
If necessary, create a new additional EDM reference system with the name from project
A on an existing EDM reference system origin.
• The world workplanes in project A as well as in project B must be set to current for
copying in both projects, otherwise the electrode with the machine head will be inserted
at the wrong position.
• The EDM reference system must be identical in project A as well as in project B to the
world workplane in the Z direction, otherwise no selection of the EDM reference will be
displayed for the virtual electrodes created from the copy.
• A machine head must be present for collision checking.
Steps
In project A:
1. Select the entire group and the reference position (workplane “ElectrodeWorkplaneE-
lectrode001”) of “Electrode001”.
If the reference position is not also copied, the machine head is not checked and no
coordinate dimensioning is created during extraction.
2. Copy to the clipboard using Edit → Copy.
hyperCAD®-S
458

-- 472 of 600 --

A.hmc
In project B:
1. Paste the group and reference position of “Electrode001” from the clipboard using Edit
→ Paste.
• No collision check is carried out when pasting. This electrode is not checked!
• The inserted “Electrode001” serves only as a “master” for new positions of virtual
electrodes.
• Virtual electrodes that have been generated are collision checked again if the EDM
reference and the machine are present.
B.hmc
2. Use the Virtual electrode command for “Electrode001” to check whether virtual electro-
des of the original project A still exist. If necessary, delete these virtual electrodes.
3. Check the description of “Electrode001” using the Modify EDM parameters command.
hyperCAD®-S
459

-- 473 of 600 --

4. Copy “Electrode001” to the new positions in project B.
When copying, the machine head must be visible as a preview if it was created correct-
ly with the master. Otherwise, the machine or the reference position will be missing.
5. In the Virtual electrode command, select the “Electrode001” master. Change the “Cop-
ied” property to the “Virtual” property for its electrode copies located in the new posi-
tion. Select the appropriate EDM reference system.
5
6. If necessary, hide the “Electrode001” master.
7. Select the virtual electrodes for export without the “Electrode001” master in the Export
reports command.
IMPORTANT
The “Electrode001” pasted from the clipboard in project B is no longer
collision checked, although it contains the status “collision-free”. Only the
newly created virtual electrodes are checked for collisions.

Raw material size
X, Y or Diameter and Z, and EDM erosion area: When changing the raw material size of the
solid of an electrode (by means of direct modelling, for instance), the changed values are
highlighted with a new background colour, and by using Apply, the values in the electrode
TAGs are updated.
NOTE
These changes must always be applied using Modify EDM parameters,
otherwise they are no longer included in the process.
Example 19. Changing a raw material that is assigned to an electrode
The designation of a solid as a raw material is carried out using the name Raw material
of the solid. The raw material must be assigned to the electrode and correctly coloured.
The shape of the raw material (cuboid or cylinder) must remain the same. The size must
be chosen in such a way that the raw material or the resulting block still fits the electrode
holder.
In case a raw material needs to be slightly adjusted in size:
hyperCAD®-S
461

-- 475 of 600 --

1. Select the solid with the name Raw material in the model tree inside the electrode and
Unhide it.
2. Adjust the shape of the raw material via direct modelling. Make sure that the group and
the solid are open for the selection of the desired face.
3. Select this solid and Hide it.
4. Check that the Raw material size in Modify EDM parameters has been updated cor-
rectly. Update the values in the electrode TAGs by using Apply. A message in the Info
tab confirms the successful update.
In case a raw material has been unintentionally erased:
1. Set the layer of the electrode as the current construction layer by using Set current
layer.
2. Activate the colour assigned in File → Options → Electrode options under Colours →
Raw material - when using the presets, this is the colour ‘Electrode 08 raw material’.
3. Construct the new raw material as a solid.
4. Move this solid to the electrode group by using Edit → Group / Ungroup.
5. Select this solid and enter the name Raw material under Context menu → Edit → Assign
name.
6. Select this solid and Hide it.
7. Check that the Raw material size in Modify EDM parameters has been updated cor-
rectly. Update the values in the electrode TAGs by using Apply. A message in the Info
tab confirms the successful update.

Enter your search text. Matching results will be shown as the text is entered. Confirm your
input with the ENTER key. The search history is then saved temporarily. In order to show
multiple different content items at the same time, use space characters to separate text
fragments. The search command is not case sensitive.
Clear: Remove the search string again. To do this, right-click the line to display the context
menu: Select the command there.

Modify eroding path
Make eroding path visible and modify.
Electrodes → Modify eroding path
EDMcon
2022.1
In order to influence the movement of the electrode through the workpiece and the erosion
movement during the erosion process, make the eroding path visible and modify it.
An electrode with an eroding path can be neither copied nor moved.
For Blind and Through, the C angle must be added (if defined during creation). When the
electrode is created, the C angle only contains 3 points.
If the reference position of the electrode for the target position is on the contact face, no
C-axis rotation can be defined. The axis of the machine does not match the axis of the
electrode. The rotation of the electrode is automatically inserted at the first point of the
selected curves at the beginning of the eroding movement when the curves of the eroding
path are selected for Blind or Through.
Select
Electrode: Select an electrode. The electrode name is shown. The existing eroding path is
made visible.
NOTE
If an electrode cannot be selected, check in Virtual electrode whether it is
because it is a copied electrode. Extract the electrode, if necessary.
If a master electrode can be selected, then it is only to simulate the
eroding path. It is not possible to change the eroding path.
Extract eroding path. The path information stored for the electrode is output as curves for
purposes such as inserting dimensions or changing the eroding path using these curves.
3 points
The electrode is moved back and forth along the same eroding path from three positions.
The initial situation is the situation after the generation of an electrode.
hyperCAD®-S
463

-- 477 of 600 --

Clearance position: Change the X, Y and Z coordinates of this electrode position.
Start position: Change the X, Y and Z coordinates of this electrode position.
Target position: The X, Y and Z coordinates of the electrode target position are displayed
and cannot be changed.
Blind
The electrode is moved back and forth along the same eroding path from more than three
positions. Construct an electrode suitable for this purpose. Construct waypoints suitable for
this purpose and connect them with curves. The curves are applied as eroding paths. Hide
these points.
IMPORTANT
Place the electrode in the target position of the eroding path. It is usual-
ly conveniently generated in this position. The curves only have to be
selected once and are used for both directions of movement.
If only a single line is selected, then that line is reset to 3 points with the C angle that was
assigned when the electrode was created. This can also be used to define a new target
position.

Motion
Curves: Select curves that represent the path of the electrode 4 	. Open the command
Reset in the context menu to discard an existing eroding path. This allows the selection of
the required curves to be started again.
In addition to lines, arcs can also be selected as eroding paths. The arcs must be parallel to
the EDM reference in XY planes.
Invert: Invert the direction of the electrode movement 5 	.
Add point C-axis rotation
Point: Rotate the electrode at an angle around the C-axis at one position. Select the blue
marker at the beginning or end of the respective curve segment as the Point 6 	.
In the graphic area, enter an angle for the rotation around the C-axis directly at the selected
point 7 	. The angles of the individual turns are not summed. For example, for a further
rotation at three points, each by 90°, enter the angle values 90°, 180° and 270°. The
direction of rotation is determined by the preceding sign + / -. Plus turns in accordance with
the right-hand rule, anticlockwise. Minus turns clockwise. In the NC program, a single-step,
180° rotation at a point is divided into two steps. For example, 0° - 90° - 180° or 10° - 100° -
190°.
Add synchronous C-axis rotation
Point: Rotate the electrode continuously at an angle around the C-axis between two posi-
tions synchronously with the forward movement. Select the yellow marker in the middle of
the respective curve segment as the Point 6 	.
In the graphic area, enter an angle for the rotation around the C-axis directly at the point
in the middle of the curve segment 7 	. The angles of the individual turns are not summed.
For example, for a continuous rotation along six segments by 45° each, enter the angle
values 45°, 90°, 135°, 180°, 225° and 270°. The direction of rotation is determined by the
preceding sign + / -. Plus turns in accordance with the right-hand rule, anticlockwise. Minus
turns clockwise.
hyperCAD®-S
465

-- 479 of 600 --

Tolerance: The rotary movement is implemented in the NC program by subdividing into
G1 segments and partial angles. Control the accuracy of the subdivision with the tolerance
(approximate chordal tolerance).

Collision check
Simulation Center: Open the
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center and perform a motion simu-
lation with checking for collision between workpiece and electrode.2
The selection of the machine is carried out by using the Machine head for collision check
option in the electrode options. The file DIN_ISO.mdf is provided as an ‘empty’ preset for an
eroding machine. It is always used when there is no complete machine model *.vmm of an
eroding machine available. To obtain such machine models, contact an OPEN MIND sales
partner. Instructions for managing the eroding machines during simulation can be found in
section Preparing the eroding machine (page 486).
The entities selected in the Create reference system (page 442) command under Entities
and assigned to the electrode via the selected reference system are used as a workpiece.
The graphical representation of the eroding path in the simulation is placed on the lowest
point of the electrode in the Z axis direction (like with the milling cutter tip) and not on the
reference point of the electrode. The values displayed by the NC program correspond to the
actual erosion coordinates.
2Not available in
hyperCAD®-S Viewer and
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer. A simulation is possible with electrodes created in
hyperCAD®-S versions 2019.1 and
later.
hyperCAD®-S
470

-- 484 of 600 --

hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center - Quick overview of operation
Start simulation.
Switch on collision check.
In the event of a collision, the electrode is coloured red, and the model is
coloured purple. No distinction is made as to whether the collision is due
to intentional workpiece contact (at the target position) or due to an actual
collision as a result of touching or penetrating the workpiece, such as dur-
ing rotation around the C-axis. The detected collisions must be analysed
individually.
Further information on operation can be found in the
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center help
section.

Export reports
Export electrode and raw material summaries.
Electrodes → Export reports
Electrode
Output electrode and raw material reports for the electrodes of the document 1 	.
The reports are saved according to the settings specified in the File → Options → Electrode
options command in the Directories branch. When using the EDM converter software to
convert electrode reports into the formats used by other eroding machine manufacturers, it
is advisable to use the Export reports command to select a directory for permanent use.
The reports generated using the templates electrode_summary.xml or full_electrode_sum-
mary.xml contain information that indicates whether the referenced erosion path shape is 3
points, Blind or Through. The waypoint coordinates and rotation angles of eroding paths are
not given in these reports.
Format
Determine the purpose or format of the report by selecting a template 2 	.
In this template, set the flag attribute to 1 3 	to write the required information to the reports
4 	.
In order to be offered an *.xml template for selection, it must be stored in the Summaries
subdirectory of the directory specified in the Electrode options, in electrode holders and
XML files. The template for the *.xslt file must also be saved at this point.
An *.xslt file 5 	is also output to this directory. This file is evaluated in order to format the
data when a report is opened in a browser 8 	or in table management. A custom logo 6
and a stylesheet *.css 7 	can be added to this template.

NOTICE
The EDM converter only supports eroding paths with the 3 points shape.
Blind and Through are not currently supported. The inclusion of support
for these eroding path shapes is being worked on for one of the next
releases.
Procedure
1. In preparation, it is advisable to go into
hyperCAD®-S File → Options → Electrode
options and specify a directory that will be used permanently for the transfer of the re-
ports. To do this, enter a directory in Directories → Export reports. Select this directory
in the EDM converter software in Directories → Import.
2. Finish preparing electrodes with
hyperCAD®-S Electrode, then:
a. Open the Electrodes → Export reports command. Select the Export for EDM con-
verter option. The option can only be selected with an EDMcon license. Data from
the EDM converter software is output into theEDM_Converter_summary_2.0.xml
template.
b. Enter a File name for the Electrode summary. The file must contain the file exten-
sion “ElectrodeSummary” as a naming convention, so that a file can be saved with
a name such as My_project.ElectrodeSummary.xml.
c. Select all the required electrodes in the Electrode list. Execute the command.
3. Start the EDM converter software.
4. The reports are already read in when opening from the first file found if one or more
files with the *.ElectrodeSummary.xml file extension are found in the selected Import
directory. They are listed on the left. If no reports are found, check the directory and the
file extension. Reload the electrodes.
5. If necessary, select another report from the drop-down menu.
6. In Machine, select an eroding machine and, if necessary, adjust other machine-specific
settings, for example, the position of the clearance plane.
7. Perform the data conversion. To do this, click the button with the green tick in the
middle. The directory specified in Export is used for the output. The result is listed
on the right. Enter a name for the converted report in File name. The individual part
number is suggested.
8. If another report is to be converted, remove the left and right lists using File → Clear
hyperCAD-S electrodes. Select another report from the drop-down menu.
Directories
Import: Change the preset directories, if necessary.
On the context menu it is possible to copy, cut and paste file paths via the
clipboard.
Export: Change the preset directories, if necessary.
On the context menu it is possible to copy, cut and paste file paths via the
clipboard.
Look in the import directory that has been set.
Look in the export directory that has been set.
Select a report from the drop-down menu.
Reload electrodes: Reload the specified report.
hyperCAD®-S
478

If the import directory has been changed while the command is being used, the

Electrode holder and raw material editor
Manage electrode holders and raw materials.

Electrode
The electrode holder and raw material editor is a separate program named blockdataedi-
tor.exe in the
hyperCAD®-S program directory and it is used to manage the combinations of
electrode holders and raw material that exist in production.

trode holder file. Remove an electrode holder from the library using Remove raw material
and electrode holder.

using Remove raw material.
Double-click with the left mouse button in the field of the appropriate column to fill out or
change values. Or call the command Edit on the context menu.

holder 1 	.

Entering X means the X axis lies in the direction of the mount.
Entering Y means the Y axis lies in the direction of the mount.
Not entering anything means there is no principal orientation.

IMPORTANT
Electrode holders are used in the collision model for the collision check.
The electrode holders are surrounded by a bounding box for each solid,
which are used as the basis for collision checking.
Configure the model geometry for the electrode holder:
• Optimise the level of model detail for precision and calculation time. To do so, prepare
the electrode holder geometry.
• Place the world workplane centred on the support surface of the raw material in the
electrode holder 2 	.
• Orient the electrode holder so that the electrode mount (e.g., holes or slot) lies in direc-
tion of the X or Y axis (principal orientation).

Preparing electrode holder geometry
Tips to prepare electrode holder geometry, as they can be imported as 3D geometry from
manufacturers.
Data optimisation is necessary. Every solid in an electrode holder is used for the collision
check. If the data is not prepared, more time is needed to check and insert. This can be
avoided:
1. Reduce the geometry to the key information about the electrode holder needed for the
collision check. Delete all entities except the holder faces ('shell').
Edit → Delete
2. Check for redundant entities. Delete double faces.
Analysis → Check double entities
3. Convert NURBS entities to analytical geometry.
Modify → Convert to analytical
4. Merge 'fragmented' faces into an entity Simplify the faces.
Modify → Simplify
hyperCAD®-S
482

-- 496 of 600 --

5. Create solids. Create as few solids as possible. Only the characteristic base shapes
are captured as a solid. A maximum of 3 solids is recommended.
Shapes → Create solid
Command line parameters for the ‘Blockdataeditor’ program
-i Enable large preview images.
-o [PATH/NAME.xml] Open the specified *.xml file. Can also be specified at the end of the
parameters or as a single parameter without -o.
-d [PATH] Set the start directory.
-l [language code] Use the specified language instead of the locale.
User-defined orbit type
Define your own orbit types for a machine.
The orbit types are offered for selection in Create, Create user defined and in Modify EDM
parameters as well as in the Electrode options and are used directly by the EDM converter
and forwarded to the eroding machine.
The selection of an orbit type is based on a user-defined file Electrode_orbit.xml in the
directory %MYDIR%/hyperCAD-S/files/electrode. It contains the displayed name of the <or-
bit> orbit types of the name attribute. The description attribute can be used for remarks,
such as for the machine in question.
The T_ORBIT tag is assigned the content of the name attribute that is passed to the EDM
converter.
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMOrbits>
<!-- Machine A -->
<orbit name="Sink" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Sphere" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Square" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Widen" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Linear" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Sink and widen" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Sink and shpere" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Sink and square" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Injection" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="Half sphere" description="Machine A" />
<orbit name="ISOG" description="Machine A" />
<!-- Machine B -->
<orbit name="Sink" description="Machine B" />
</EDMOrbits>
User-defined material list
Define a separate material list for workpieces and for eroding with electrodes in the file
default.densities.xml as a default setting in ...\files\materials\.... The modified file, like all
modified files, should be copied to the roaming or company directory and not modified in the
installation directory, for example, in C:\Users\[USER]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND\hy-
perCAD-S\[VERSION]\materials\default.densities.xml.
Materials for workpieces
Enter the name, description and mass density properties of a material in <matter> with the
attributes Name, description and massDensity. Enter the name of the material group in title
within <category>. The unit of measure can be changed globally in <categories> with the
attribute measureUnit and with the values lb/ft³ or kg/dm³. In case of problems, use the
hyperCAD®-S
483

-- 497 of 600 --

standard escape sequences lb/&amp;#179; or kg/dm&amp;#179. Optionally, also define the
unit of measure for each individual material:
<matter name="0.1234"
description="EN-GJMW-1234"
massDensity="123.4567"
measureUnit="lb/ft³" />
<matter name="0.1234"
description="EN-GJMW-1234"
massDensity="123.4567"
measureUnit="lb/ft&#179;" />
The materials are offered for selection in Edit → Assign material, Electrodes → Modify EDM
parameters and in Electrodes → Print.

<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
<Materials version="2019.2">
<categories measureUnit="kg/dm³" context="materials">
<category title="Aluminum - alloys">
<matters>
<matter name="3.0205"
description="EN-AW 1200"
massDensity="2.700" />
<matter name="3.0255"
description="EN-AW 1350A"
massDensity="2.700" />
<matter name="3.0505"
description="EN-AW 3105"
massDensity="2.710" />
</matters>
</category>
<category title="Graphite">
<matters>
<matter name="ISEM-1"
description="ISEM-1"
massDensity="1.680" />
<matter name="TTK-50"
description="TTK-50"
massDensity="1.800" />
<matter name="TTK-4"
description="TTK-4"
massDensity="1.780" />
</matters>
</category>
</categories>

Preparing eroding machine heads
In order to include a head of a die-sinking EDM machine in the collision check, a sufficiently
precise geometric description must be stored and managed. Record all machines to a file
electrode_machine.xml that are to be available for machine selection. This file contains
references to *.hmc-Documents with geometries of the machine heads.
Under .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode/Machine, save the *.hmc documents with the geome-
tries:
Split the geometry into layers in accordance with the command. Position the workplane
so that the electrode holder fits the adapter and the correct length of the combination is
obtained.
• On one layer with the name from the XML entity <CAxisLayer>: All solids that adequately
describe the clamping system that holds the electrode holder. The clamping system is
rotated like the electrode holder according to the C angle.
• All remaining layers: All solids that describe the machine axis with sufficient accuracy.
The alignment refers to the reference system and is static.
Under .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode, save the electrode_machine.xml file with the following
structure.
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>
<EDMMachine name="Machine name">
<filename>Machine\Machinename.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>Clamping device</CAxisLayer>
</EDMMachine>
</EDMMachines>
Enter the name of the eroding machine under <EDMMachine> in the name attribute . This
name is displayed in the dialogue for selection. In <filename>, enter the directory and the
hyperCAD®-S
485

-- 499 of 600 --

*.hmc document that contains the geometry data. In <CAxisLayer>, enter the layer name
containing all solids of the clamping system for the electrode holder.
Example 22. XML file with two die sinking EDM machines and the same clamping system
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>
<EDMMachine name="S GE 400">
<filename>Machine\SGE400.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>S3R</CAxisLayer>
</EDMMachine>
<EDMMachine name="CM 323 B">
<filename>Machine\CM323B.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>S3R</CAxisLayer>
</EDMMachine>
</EDMMachines>
IMPORTANT
The machine models supplied are examples and should not be used for
collision checking in production.
Theelectrode_machine.xml file is required even without a machine. The content can be
reduced to the following:
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>
</EDMMachines>
The electrode reports show whether the machine head has been checked for collisions.
IMPORTANT
If the machine is changed within the Modify EDM parameters command,
the machine head is no longer checked for collisions.
The machine head is set to not checked if the machine name is changed.
The machine head is displayed in the preview, but is not added to the
model.
Preparing the eroding machine
In order to include a die-sinking EDM machine head in collision avoidance when generating
an electrode, a sufficiently precise geometric description must be stored and managed.
Record all machines that are to be available for selection in the Machine head for collision
check option in a electrode_machine.xml file. This file contains references to *.hmc docu-
ments containing geometries of the machine heads.
For a motion simulation that includes an eroding path collision check with a complete erod-
ing machine model generated using the Modify eroding path command, which is located
in the
hyperMILL® SIMULATION Center, include further references to *.vmm files in the
electrode_machine.xml file.
hyperCAD®-S
486

-- 500 of 600 --

Under .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode/machine, save the *.hmc documents with the geome-
tries:
Split the geometry into layers in accordance with the command. Position the workplane
so that the electrode holder fits the adapter and the correct length of the combination is
obtained.
• On one layer with the name from the XML entity <CAxisLayer>: All solids that adequately
describe the clamping system that holds the electrode holder. The clamping system is
rotated like the electrode holder according to the C angle.
• All remaining layers: All solids that describe the machine axis with sufficient accuracy.
The alignment refers to the reference system and is static.
Under .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode/machine save the *.vmm file with the complete eroding
machine model. To obtain such machine models, contact an OPEN MIND sales partner.
The files located in the .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode/simulation directory do not have to
be adapted and must not be changed. These files are required for the respective concrete
simulation and are processed automatically.
Under .../hyperCAD-S/files/electrode, save the electrode_machine.xml file with the following
structure.
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>
<EDMMachine name="Machine name">
<filename>Machine\Machinename.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>Clamping device</CAxisLayer>
<SimulationModelFile>Machine\Machinename.vmm
</SimulationModelFile>
</EDMMachine>
</EDMMachines>
Enter the name of the eroding machine under <EDMMachine> in the name attribute. This
name is displayed for selection in the dialogue. Under <filename>, enter the directory
and the *.hmc document that contains the geometry data for the machine head. Under
<CAxisLayer>, enter the layer name containing all solids for the electrode holder’s clamping
system.
Under <SimulationModelFile>, enter the *.vmm file with the EDM machine’s complete ma-
chine model.
Example 23. XML file with two die sinking EDM machines and the same clamping system
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>
<EDMMachine name="S GE 400">
<filename>Machine\SGE400.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>S3R</CAxisLayer>
<SimulationModelFile></SimulationModelFile>
</EDMMachine>
<EDMMachine name="CM 323 B">
<filename>Machine\CM323B.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>S3R</CAxisLayer>
<SimulationModelFile></SimulationModelFile>
</EDMMachine>
</EDMMachines>
Example 24. XML file with die-sinking EDM machine for simulation
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<EDMMachines>

487

-- 501 of 600 --

<EDMMachine name="ERODICON_EDM01">
<filename>Machine\Erodicon_01.hmc</filename>
<CAxisLayer>Layer_0001</CAxisLayer>
<SimulationModelFile>Machine\Erodicon_01.vmm</SimulationModelFile>
</EDMMachine>
</EDMMachines>

Milling areas
Faces that are used in milling areas are protected from being modified by the user. The
Unlock entities command can be used to disable this protection so that it is possible to edit
these faces.
A V-sketch can be selected as a machining contour. It is used as a closed polygonal line
for job calculations. If the V-sketch is changed (for example, fillets or chamfers are inserted)
and the contour line remains closed, the machining contour of the job is updated. However,
this only applies if the V-sketch was generated from Version 2021.1.
Coordinate system
Set WP to frame: Create a coordinate system for multi-side machining (
hyperMILL® Frame)
once or several times for a workplane in the same position and orientation.
Link frame to Wp: For an associative workplane in the same position and orientation, create
a coordinate system for multi-side machining (
hyperMILL®) once and link it to this selected
workplane. Only available if parametric modelling is switched on and the workplane is
associative.
Example 25. How to Link a coordinate system for multi-side machining (
hyperMILL® Frame)
with a workplane
1. Check that the parametric modeling for
hyperCAD®-S is switched on.
2. Create a workplane, e.g. with the command Workplane → On face. Select the option
Associative and enter a name for the workplane.
3. There are two ways to link the workplane to a frame:
• If the frame already exists:
In
hyperMILL®, select a frame and call the Edit command, in Frame definition →
Definition select the option Associative workplane and in the drop-down menu select
the relevant associative workplane from
hyperCAD®-S.
• If a new frame is required:
In
hyperCAD®-S, select the command Link frame to WP in the WP tab in the context
menu of the associative workplane. A new frame is created in the frame browser
in
hyperMILL®. The option Associative workplane is activated and the associative
workplane is selected.
4. To be able to make a change to the geometry, check whether the Activate option is
switched off in hyperMILL → Setup → Settings in Document → Locking.
Toolpath entities
Toolpath entities are managed without layer information, and do not appear in the model
tree on the Model tab. Display settings are not managed as for all CAD entities.
Make settings for toolpath entities in
hyperMILL® commands:
• hyperMILL → Setup → Settings on the Document tab in Feedbacks → Colours
• hyperMILL → Setup → Settings on the hyperCAD-S document tab in Toolpath display
• in the job dialogue on the General tab
Geometric attributes within a toolpath entity can be determined using CAD commands from
the Analysis menu. To do this, activate the Toolpath polyline-point snap filter and the Inside
toolpath snap option in the Snap selection filter.
Toolpath entities are not reflected in the visibility filter on the Visibility tab. Toolpath entities
cannot be filtered by colour and layer in the Selection filter on the Selection tab.
The Edit → Graphic properties command does not display any information on the graphic
properties of a toolpath entity and its segments.
The Edit → Copy attributes command cannot be used because a toolpath entity is managed
only by
hyperMILL®.
hyperCAD®-S
492

-- 506 of 600 --

Click a toolpath entity with the left mouse button in the graphics area to select the associ-
ated job in the
hyperMILL® browser. Double-click the
hyperMILL® job with the left mouse
button to open it for editing.
hyperMILL® commands can be run for the selected toolpath entity on the context menu:
• Select a single toolpath entity: The Modify command is shown.
• Select one or several toolpath entities: Commands that apply to toolpaths are shown.
• If no toolpath entity has been selected, no toolpath-related commands are shown.
hyperMILL® browser
Create and manage jobs, job lists and frames as well as models, features and macros in
the
hyperMILL® browser. The
hyperMILL® browser on the hyperMILL tab is used for NC
programming with
hyperMILL®. Help information is provided in
hyperMILL® help.
• References Exposure: A method to permanently mark the geometry and boundaries of
jobs and job list used for toolpath calculation. Note, only available for
hyperMILL® in
hyperCAD®-S!
Q key: Switch on marking. Area is visually highlighted. Other geometry is displayed
transparently. In a job list, information/entities of all jobs involved are displayed. When
switching to a single job, job-related information/entities are automatically adjusted.
The tooltip of a referenced entity displays the assigned job.
The visibility filter and the selection filter are taken into account. The Check visibility
command can help to determine and switch off the visibility settings of entities.
Select the colour for referenced entities in File → Options → Options / properties in the
Graphic → System → Rendering → Job referenced entities colour option.

Display coordinate values of toolpath points in relation to a reference coordinate system.

-- 507 of 600 --

Select the toolpath to be analysed in the
hyperMILL® job browser, for example, by ena-
bling/disabling using the icon.
The tooltip can be used to display properties of a toolpath point by resting the cursor on

toolpath command is active. There, drag the tags for the toolpath analysis to the right
column to activate them. Also see Tooltip content (page 41).
Visibility
Enable the visualisation of toolpath properties in the graphics area:
Feedrates by colour map: Display the feedrate values of the toolpaths with different colours
1 	. Settings on the Colour map tab.
Points: Show the toolpath points.
Vectors: Display the tool vectors for 5-axis orientations in the toolpath points in the graphics
area.
Jobinfo: Display the name of the toolpath from the job information in the graphics area.
Directions: Display the direction of the machining in the graphics area – an arrow is shown
on the toolpath point.
In the case of checkboxes with three switch states, the settings from job settings are
used for the 'indefinite' state. The representation is therefore visible without a running
toolpath analysis.
Limit toolpath
Limit the analysis of toolpaths to a smaller toolpath area. The slide controls can be moved
to the left or to the right. To do this, hold the left mouse button down on the slide control.
The analysis area that has been limited in this way can be moved further along the toolpath.
To do this, hold the left mouse button down between the two slide controls. If the slide
controls overlap, move the mouse to the middle and keep it held down there.
Select the beginning of an area to be analysed. To do this, click a toolpath point.
Select the end of an area to be analysed. To do this, click a toolpath point.
Limiting the analysis area does not affect the Internal simulation in
hyperMILL®. The simula-
tion also takes place outside of the limited analysis area.
Values
Select
Analyse the toolpath points or the movement between toolpath points. The analysis can
be carried out for different parts within one or between two different toolpaths. Measure
distances between toolpath points, toolpath movements, points, curves and faces.
Click on a toolpath point to select between two such points for a toolpath movement.
Info: Select a part (toolpath point or movement). Use the arrow buttons to go to the next or
previous toolpath part.
Measure: Optionally select a second part or an entity.
Reference coordinate system
Specify a coordinate system for the analysis. Select from NCS, Frame and WP.

Limit feedrate

Min.: Manually change the smallest feedrate to be analysed for the colour map of the

are possible. Otherwise, the number of intervals is determined automatically using the
existing feedrates. The number of currently existing colours for feedrate values or feedrate
intervals is displayed at the end of the line.
The colour map automatically switches to the display in feedrate intervals if more than 100
individual feedrates exist in the toolpaths to be analysed.
Colour map
A table showing the assignment of the colours to the feedrate intervals is displayed. The
colours are assigned automatically and cannot be changed. The table is sorted from the
largest to the smallest feedrate interval. Feedrates outside of the user-defined limits are
shown in white (larger) and black (smaller).

Analyse distance toolpath-shape
Analyse distances between surfaces and a toolpath.
hyperMILL → Analyse distance toolpath-shape
2023
The distances between a toolpath and surfaces are determined. A subarea of the toolpath
can be selected using a selection area. Two target distances divide the toolpath into three
steps. Points can be created at which the toolpath meets the required distances.
For the analysis, a toolpath calculated with a ballmill is assumed and displayed for the tool
centre point. This is not checked.
To perform a local analysis, position the mouse at the desired location. A shortest distance
to a next face is calculated, and the value and a line are displayed.
Automatic computation: After selecting all entities, start the calculation for the analysis. The
results can be applied only once the calculation of the preview has been carried out.

Min. and Max. display the determined minimum and maximum values.
The X axis shows a colour map and assigns a colour to each value in the analysis area.
A colour symbolises the value assigned to the analysed toolpath. The Y axis provides
qualitative information about the scope of the respective value in relation to all determined

analysis area to its original size. Auto calculates an optimal analysis area.
The value ‘inf’ is displayed when no result was determined, or if the result goes to infinity.

hyperMILL® itself already provides powerful options for automation through base com-

hyperMILL® AUTOMATION Center. The objective is

Existing reference
Face: A calculation on the basis of a plane is predefined as the reference. To manage
undercuts, select a reference face that is shaped differently. It must be possible to project
the boundaries onto the reference face or the face is larger than the projected area.
Parameter
The following options may be active or inactive, depending on the mode selected.
Density: Set a density for the tessellation of the face group. The selection is limited to
integers from 1 (large) to 10 (small).
Sampling points: Set a factor for the selection of the best tessellation points. The selection
is limited to integers from 1 (large) to 10 (small).
Smoothing factor: Enter a factor for the smoothing around the best points in the inner area
of the selected face group. The selection is limited to integers from 1 (small) to 10 (large).
The larger the factor, the smoother the result. However, it will also be further away from the
original shape of the face formation.
Extension (%): Enlarge the deformation space greater than the size of the target face.
Tangency tolerance check: Enter the maximum tangential deviation in the selected face for-
mation, between the adjacent faces. If you enter 0, only transitions that are really tangential
will be permitted. If the value is not 0, domes may also occur within the face formation. The
edged transitions as well as the corners are then lost as a result of smoothing.
Sewing tolerance: Enter the permissible size of face gaps within all selected faces for which
face boundaries are still regarded as continuous.
Sampling method: Perform the calculation based on the existing tessellation (option: Tes-
sellation) or based on a temporarily created grid with evenly sized triangles (option: Uniform
sampling).
Tangent to external boundaries: The resulting face should run tangentially to the outer
boundaries of the selected face formation.
Trim result: Trim the resulting face.
NURBS output
Regular parametrisation: Create a face with a regular distribution of all face parameters (in
Fast mode).
Tolerance specifies a positional conversion tolerance for the NURBS entities. Use Max.
degree to specify the maximum degree for the NURBS entities, Min. cont. to define the
minimum continuity and Max. patches to specify the maximum number of face patches.
The Min. continuity is limited to a maximum of C1. Select between 0 or 1. Max. patches
must be at least 3.

Extension faces
Create extension faces - for milling as well.
Shapes → Extension faces
2022.1
Use additional faces to create extensions that do not belong to the model geometry and can
be used for purposes such as milling face extensions and stop faces for NC programming.
Select
Faces: Select the face area to be extended 2 	. The number of selected entities is displayed.
2 7
3
Boundaries: Select the boundaries or edges where an extension is to be made 1 	. If several
boundaries are selected, filling faces are created between the faces. Mixing the selection
of faces and boundaries is not possible. The Milling mode cannot be used. The number of
selected boundaries is displayed.
3
71
Extend faces
Create extension faces directly on selected faces and boundaries.
The extension consists of additional, new tangential faces 7 	.
Two line segments are calculated for each selected boundary/edge (one at each end). Both
lines project tangentially out of the face. The face extension is generated by moving the first
line along the boundary/edge until the second line is reached. Here, a tangential transition
is maintained to the existing face along the boundary or edge.
hyperCAD®-S
506

-- 520 of 600 --

Corner extensions are calculated with G1 continuity (tangency).
Extension width: Enter the extension width 3 	.
Normal direction: An extension is automatically calculated from the direction of the adjacent
boundary of the given faces. In a solid, this is the common edge with the adjacent solid
face. As this direction can be oblique, the width of the extension is often less than the
length entered. Enable the Normal direction option to calculate an extension normal to the
selected edge or boundary 8 	.
Wider blending: If a chain of boundaries / edges has been selected and there are usable
face extensions before and after a gap, the Fill algorithm will attempt to close the gap.
If this filling face is also unusable, further adjacent extensions faces are removed and
approximated by the Fill algorithm. The warning ‘Some faces have been replaced by an
approximation’ appears. Click the warning to highlight the approximated faces in colour. To
remove the highlighting, click the empty area below the warnings.
In some cases, this approach can produce poor results. Then, switch off the Wider blending
option to fill the gap manually.
1
8
Tolerance: The tolerance is required to find the free (open) edges and to orientate between
selected and adjacent faces.

Check for undercuts
Use the Check undercuts option to determine whether an undercut in the faces to be
created results from a tool approach direction due to the orientation of the surfaces to be
processed.
Use face normals: If this option is disabled (default mode), the existing milling face normals
are not evaluated. It is generally assumed that they are all oriented ‘upwards’. If undercuts
occur in the faces to be created, an error message is displayed.
If this option is enabled, it is assumed that the milling faces are already oriented as inten-
ded. The orientation of the created faces results accordingly. If they point ‘downwards’, they
are considered to be correct.

Dimensions and labels
The dimensions of the entities are associative, such as for direct modelling. The measure
will be adjusted when the geometry is changed. The dimension can assume different states.
The state is visualised in the model tree, on the Model tab.
Table 84. Possible states with associative dimensioning
State Icon Explanation
Fully linked In the case of associative dimensioning, the modification
of entities, such as for direct modelling, will cause the
measure of the dimension to be changed accordingly.
Partially linked The assignment of a dimension to entities is one-sided.
The dependent entity is not modified.
Not linked The dimension is not related to entities, for example,
because the entities have been deleted.
hyperCAD®-S
512

-- 526 of 600 --

In terms of function, this associative dimension that can be used for V-sketches must be
distinguished from the usable dimension, in which case a modification to the measure will
adjust the geometry automatically.

Zoom
Change the display size of the text in the text input area if the text no longer fits in the text

Table 89. Zoom commands
Icon Command Explanation
Zoom in Enlarge text display
Zoom out Reduce text display
Zooming is also possible with CTRL + mouse wheel.
Rotation
Align the text box by entering a value under Angle or by dragging the handles. Use the
Direction option to orient the alignment of the text box to an entity to be selected. If the
Parallel to view option is selected, the text will always be displayed horizontally and parallel
to the view, even if the view changes.
Frame
Select a Type 1 	. Specify a Colour 2 	. Use the Diameter option 3 	to specify the space
between the text and frame for circular frames. The size of the frame remains constant
until the text reaches the frame size. Only then is the size automatically adapted to the
content. The Distance option 4 	also creates a gap between the text and frame. However,
the frame is always adapted to the size of the content. The frame only follows the shape of
the reference frame if you select the Text box option. Use Line thickness 5 	to change the
line thickness of the frame.

Text line
Enter or modify a line of text along a reference entity.
Drafting → Text line
Enter or modify a line of text along a reference entity (straight line, arched curve or circle
segment).
How are special characters entered in a text line?
To insert a special character in a text line, copy the character directly using the clipboard.
You can list all characters by entering “charmap” at the Windows command prompt, for
example.
Are engraving fonts available?
In the Text line command, fonts can be selected that may be suitable for NC programming
for engraving. The names of these fonts begin with the identifier TGS_.
Other engraving fonts (single line fonts) can be obtained over the Internet, either for a fee or
free of charge.
Modes
Line generates text between two points. Click with the left mouse button to create the start
point. Click again to specify the end point and the handles 1 	.
Arc generates text along an arched curve. Clicking with the left mouse button generates the
start point. Clicking again creates the end point. The curvature can be varied with the cursor
on the curve. Click again to display the handles 2 	.
Sector generates text along a circle segment. Clicking with the left mouse button generates
the centre of the circle. Clicking again creates the radius and the start point of the segment.
The length of the segment can be varied with the cursor on the curve. Click again to display
the handles 3 	.

Text
Enter or modify a line of text. The entry remains temporarily in the selection list for reuse
until the document is exited.
If the text line is too small due to the current zoom factor and can therefore not be read,
the text can be displayed using the tooltip. To do this, rest the cursor on the text line. This
requires that the tooltip content is adjusted. The tag text content must be transferred for
display in the tooltip content.
The text can be oriented using a horizontal or vertical snap line.
You can also modify a text line imported from the *.e3 file format.

How can a dimension be changed?
To change the position of several dimension entities, select the Edit → Move text / dimen-
sion command.
For a single modification, select the dimension entity and open the context menu using the
right mouse button.
To change the position of the dimension entity, select the Move dimensions command
or double-click the dimension with the left mouse button. To move the arrow head, double-
click the tip.
Use the Edit dimensions command on the context menu to modify further attributes of
the dimension entity. All entities created during a specific work process are handled as a
“family” of dimensions. Selecting one entity in this family will activate all entities for the
purpose of modifying attributes. The attributes of an individual entity in a “family” can be
modified individually using the Entity properties command.

Plain: For unformatted, single-line information, select the Plain option 1 	. Enter the text
information in the attributes under Plain text > Text.
Formatted: For multi-line, formatted text, select the Formatted option 2 	. Use the Input text
command to open the simplified dialogue for the Text command. Enter and format your text.
Use the handles to change the size of the text area 4 	.

Measure

Alignment of the distance
For Measure → Distance, specify whether the distances should be applied in a horizontal or
vertical direction.
Horizontal: Apply distances horizontally. Place the measure line at the required position 4 	.

Angle 0°
For Measure → Angle, specify whether the angle dimension with 0° should begin in the + X
axial direction or the + Y axial direction.
X axis: Angle 0° in X axial direction 6 	.
Y axis: Angle 0° in Y axial direction 7 	.
Angle - direction
For Measure → Angle, specify whether the angle dimension should run in a clockwise or
anticlockwise direction.
Counter clockwise: The angles are applied anticlockwise, in a ‘mathematically positive
direction of rotation’ 9 	.
Clockwise: The angles are applied clockwise, in a ‘mathematically negative direction of
rotation’ 10 	.

Surface finish
Re-enter or change the surface finish for faces.
Drafting → Surface finish
Re-enter or change the surface finish for faces. No dimensions are generated. The informa-
tion is added to the attributes of the selected faces. This command does not change an
existing roughness symbol belonging to the face in a dimension.
Specify default settings in File → Options → Drafting properties in the Surface finish section.
By importing, existing roughness specifications for roughness symbols can be modified
using the Analysis → Entity properties command.
IMPORTANT
The specifications for roughness in the dimensions and the specifications
in this command and in the Entity properties arenot linked. Changes
must be made both to the roughness symbol and to the attributes of the
faces!

18. Appendix
Information for administrators
Data backup
Experience shows that data losses will always occur. It is only a matter of time before such
a loss takes place. There are many different reasons for this, for example, a power failure,
the theft of a device, a hardware defect or a user error.
Data belongs to the operating capital of a company. Missing or incomplete data can not
only lead to material damage and damage to the image of a company, great effort is also
required to recover the data. Rules on liability, contractual penalties and proper business
operation must be observed.
Users of OPEN MIND software are fully responsible for backing up data through the provi-
sion of systems and methods to avoid the loss of data.
The following four points, for example, can be used to check the data backup status:
• Are backup copies generated? Where are they stored?
• After which time interval does the data change to a relevant extent? Is the storage
frequency suitable for this?
• How quickly does the backed-up data need to be available again after a data loss?
• Is the recovery of data tested successfully on a regular basis?
Options for generating backup copies can be selected under File → Options → Options /
properties. Further information is provided in Application (page 60).
Basic information can be found on Wikipedia, for example, https://en.wikipedia.org/wi-
ki/Backup.
Application data
Each version of the software stores its application data in a separate directory under
C:\Users\[USERNAME]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND.
Crash log
If the software crashes, a crash log hmc_[DATE-TIME].crashlog will be generated and
saved to %temp%\OPEN MIND.

Graphics card and monitor
A graphics card with a latest-generation GPU and an OpenGL accelerated graphics card
driver supporting OpenGL Version 4.0 or higher is recommended for
hyperCAD®-S.
Windows does not automatically support OpenGL accelerated drivers. As a user, you
must install a manufacturer or OEM driver to be able to directly use hardware-accelerated
OpenGL. These drivers are made available on the websites of most graphics hardware
manufacturers.
Reference drivers can also be installed for notebook graphics cards. Please note that
your notebook manufacturer may offer OEM drivers for your notebook on their website.
Please check the website to see if there are any recommended software updates for your
notebook.
If you have a computer with two graphics cards, select the accelerated graphics card
(AMD / NVIDIA) instead of the integrated graphics card in the screen’s Control Panel. In the
case of NVIDIA Quadro graphics cards, we recommend that you set the ‘Workstation app -
dynamic streaming’ or ‘3D App - Visual Simulation’ profile in the NVIDIA Control Panel.
NOTE
Select the accelerated GPU (AMD / NVIDIA) instead of the integrated
GPU in the graphic card settings of a computer with two graphics cards.

The recommended graphics card drivers do not take into account the connection of addi-
tional peripheral devices. Connected devices may affect the quality and performance of the
graphics card. OPEN MIND Technologies therefore recommends contacting the manufac-

Related topics. Graphics card and monitor (page 551)
Hi-DPI system / 4K-Ultra-HD - support for high-resolution monitors
High-resolution 4K monitors may display objects as very small. Therefore,
hyperCAD®-S
sets environment variables to adjust the display and thus automatically determines the
scaling behaviour. This gives the user the benefit of not having to define these environment
variables, and it also carries the benefit that Qt-based software programmes are not affec-
ted. It is possible to override this behaviour by making manual adjustments. To do this, the
following options are available:
• Enlarge the text display. To do this, change the setting in Windows Settings → System →
Display → Change the size of texts, apps and other items.
For monitors ranging from 27” to 32” (medium sized screens), it is recommended to
adjust the font size to 150% first. For very large monitors, the font size can be increased
further, for example, to 200%. Please note that if the font size is scaled by more than
150%, the size of the dialogues is doubled.
With a large font size, some texts in the
hyperCAD®-S user interface dialogues may
appear cut off.
• In this case, the software can be configured to run in Full HD resolution compatibility
mode by setting the Overwrite high DPI scaling option for the hmc.exe to System.
To do this, right-click the hmc.exe and open Properties in the context menu. In the
Compatibility tab, select the Change High DPI Settings button. Select the Overwrite
high DPI scaling option and select the System setting from the drop-down menu.
• In order to be able to work with a 4K monitor while maintaining ultra HD resolution
and large font size, the Qt 5 framework has introduced a scaling behaviour with the
environment variable QT_AUTO_SCREEN_SCALE_FACTOR (page 561) = 1. This
option scales the Qt-UI objects, such as dialogues, buttons and icons, on high-resolu-
tion monitors. The Qt scaling behaviour does not affect the 3D rendering window (the
graphics area). It retains its resolution. Some elements may then appear too small.
The environment variable OMSL_GRAPHICS_ENABLE_PIXEL_RATIO (page 560) =
1 extends the scaling behaviour to the 3D rendering window as well (for manipulators,
snap points, workplanes, for example) using the same ratio that Qt calculates for
invariant zoom objects.
• Reduce the 4K Ultra HD resolution from 3840x2160 pixels to the Full HD resolution
of 1920x1080 pixels. To do this, change the setting in Windows Settings → System →
hyperCAD®-S
552

-- 566 of 600 --

Display → Screen Resolution. This makes all software programs appear twice as large on
the monitor. The space on the screen is reduced accordingly.
Company-wide default settings
The software settings can be managed centrally for all users. All local instances read the
required settings from various *.xml files and the *.ini files that are located in directories
specifically set up for this purpose.
Procedure
First specify and save the company settings locally in the software. All files are stored in
C:\Users\[user]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND\[Software].... Make any further changes to
the settings here. Delete any files and entries that are not required. When deleting an entry,
make sure that the XML structure remains valid. Set up a directory on a computer that acts
as the server computer and can be accessed by all local computers. Copy the customised
files and their directory structure to this directory. It is advisable to map the server as a
drive, for example, W:\Companyfolder\. After that, refer to the directory on this drive.
Set up the Windows environment variablesHC_INI_FILE (page 558), OMSL_COMPA-
NY_SETTINGS (page 558) and HC_ADDITIONAL_HCCONFIG (page 557). Then start
the software.
TIP
To display the current environment variable settings, select the Analysis
→ Document info command. The result appears on the Output tab.
hcsettingswizard.exe
hcsettingswizard.exe is a console application in the
hyperCAD®-S root folder. It is updated
with every release. It runs as a stand-alone application. It is used to transfer software
settings from one version to another, or from one computer to many others. It can be used
for every application based on
hyperCAD®-S.
In
hyperCAD®-S, it also possible to use the commands under File → Options → Layout
manager.
NOTE
This application should not be confused with the
hyperMILL® Settings
Wizard or SettingsService.exe.
When started without parameters, it copies the software settings from the last available
hyperCAD®-S version to the latest version. For other applications (for example,
hyperMILL®
TOOL Builder), specify the application at the command line. This works for both registry
parameters and roaming settings. Registry parameters are exported in a *.reg file, and
roaming settings are exported to a user-defined target folder structure.
IMPORTANT
To manage the registry parameters via a *.reg file (Parameter -f), hcset-
tingswizard.exe must be run with administrator rights.
hyperCAD®-S
553

-- 567 of 600 --

You can also export to a packed file (*.7z) both registry and roaming settings with version
information. Then you can import the settings from *.7z file. You can do automatically for
the same version or you can specify the destination version. It is up to you do the right
thing, No checks of compatibility are done.
The *.7z file contains an additional *.txt file with version information. This information is
not contained in a *.reg file or in the roaming settings folder structure itself. The roaming
settings and the *.reg file itself are independent of the version. The software settings can
be imported in any version, if the version is specified at the command line. No compatibility
checks are carried out.
In information technology, roaming describes a special method of overlaying of folder con-
tent. See the Roaming user profile Wikipedia article for more information. Can be found
under .../AppData/Roaming/OPEN MIND/hyperCAD-S/[Version number]/...
Command line parameters
hcsettingswizard [-p <Product>] [-f <File name>] [-o <Source version>] [-n <Target ver-
sion>] [-z] [-i] [-u] [-r] [--] [--Version] [-h]
Where
-p <product> Specify the application (long form --product <product>)
-f <file name> Export to / import from file with folder (long form --file <file
name>)3
-o <source version> Specify the source version. (long form --oldVersion <source
version>)4
-n <target version> Specify the target version. (long form --newVersion <target ver-
sion>)4
-z Import or export using a *.7z file; default export (long form --zip)
-i Import exported file. (long form --import)
-u Copy user data only (roaming). (long form --User)
-r Only enable copying of registry parameters. (long form --regis-
try)
-- Ignore the rest of the arguments following this flag. (long form
--ignore_rest)
--version Display version information and exit.
-h Display help information and exit. (long form --help)
Example 26. Use hcsettingswizard with command line parameter
The examples assume Version 1.0.9.2.
hcsettingswizard -p ‘hyperMILL Shop Viewer’
Copy settings (registry and user roaming) of
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer from previous
available version (e.g. 1.0.9.1) to 1.0.9.2
hcsettingswizard -p ‘hyperMILL Shop Viewer’ -r
Copy only registry settings of
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer from previous available version
(e.g. 1.0.9.1) to 1.0.9.2
hcsettingswizard -p ‘hyperMILL Shop Viewer’ -u -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings’ -o 2019.2
Copy only user roaming settings of
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer from version 2019.2 (registry
and roaming version 1.0.9.2 ) to folder c:\mydir\mysettings
hcsettingswizard -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings’ -n 1.0.9.2
3The file name is interpreted as a folder if it is used for roaming settings and files, whereby the proper file extension is added for *.reg and *.7z. In the latter
case, the extension is only added when exporting. When importing (-i), the full name with the *.7z extension must be specified.
4Specify either the internal version number e.g. 1.0.9.2 or the software version e.g. 2019.2. The software version is mapped to the internal version number, and
this is used instead. If a non-existent number is (erroneously) specified, this will be used without any checking.
The file name is interpreted as a folder for user roaming settings, and as a file name for registry parameters. In the latter case, the *.reg extension is added if
not explicitly specified.
hyperCAD®-S
554

-- 568 of 600 --

Export
hyperCAD®-S registry parameters to c:\mydir\mysettings.reg and export the user
roaming settings to the folder c:\mydir\mysettings.
hcsettingswizard -r -i -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings’ -n 1.0.9.2
Import the
hyperCAD®-S registry parameters from the c:\mydir\mysettings.reg file. The *.reg
extension is added if not specified.
hcsettingswizard -i -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings’ -n 1.0.9.2
Import
hyperCAD®-S registry parameters from the c:\mydir\mysettings.reg file and user
roaming settings from the c:\mydir\mysettings folder.
hcsettingswizard -z -f ‘hyperMILL Shop Viewer’ ‘c:\ mydir\mysettings’ -o 1.0.9.2
Export and compress
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer registry parameters and user roaming
settings to c:\mydir\mysettings.7z.
hcsettingswizard -z -i -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings.7z’
Import compressed
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer 1.0.9.2 registry parameters and user roam-
ing settings from c:\mydir\mysettings.7z. The version and application are read from the .7z
file. The version can be overwritten.
hcsettingswizard-z -i -f ‘c:\mydir\mysettings.7z’ -n 2.0.9.1
Import
hyperMILL® SHOP Viewer registry parameters and user roaming settings to Version
2.0.9.1 from c:\mydir\mysettings.7z.
NOTICE
The software version and internal version number are not checked for
validity.
Internal version numbers are, for
example,
The associated software ver-
sions are
…..
2.2.1.1 2021.1
2.2.1.2 2021.2
2.2.2.1 2022.1
2.2.2.2 2022.2
2.2.3.1 2023
2.2.3.2 2023
…..
If the software version is used, the command will search for and use the
internal version number, e.g. -o 2018.2 is converted to 1.0.8.2.
However, if e.g. -n 2018.3 is erroneously used, a new folder (registry
key) will be created with the name 2018.3. The same will happen if an
erroneous internal version number is used, e.g. -n 9.9.9.9 will create a
folder with the name 9.9.9.9.
hyperCAD®-S
555

-- 569 of 600 --

GetHS.exe for start screen
The GetHS.exe software in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\[version]\bin\...
downloads the news from the OPEN MIND server to the local workstation to %PROGRAM-
DATA%\OPEN MIND\homescreen. To ensure the security of the content, only pure HTML
2.0 code is loaded, without cascading style sheets or Javascript. The static HTML code
contains hyperlinks only to the official OPEN MIND website.
During installation, a ‘hyperCAD-S homescreen’ task is created in the Windows Task
Scheduler, with the help of which the software starts automatically every day and updates
the news area.
Press F5 to refresh the start screen. The software can also be started manually for this
purpose. The task can be changed in the Windows Task Scheduler, for example, the time
of the update can be adjusted.
The checksum (SHA256) of the GetHS.exe file is:
64B7F2225CBFC2AEB0CD386DA8FAADC5231AC442BEF68C851201399BFAA72C7A
Command line parameters
You can use command line parameters to influence the behaviour of the software.
[PATH]\hmc.exe -l [language code] starts the software with the user interface of the selec-
ted language. To use another one of the available languages, please enter the respective
language code, e.g.: fr, en, es, de, pl, nl_NL, ja, zh_cn.
[PATH]\hmc.exe -c false opens each document in a separate program instance.
[PATH]\hmc.exe -c true opens a document in the same program instance. An existing
document must be closed first.
[PATH]\hmc.exe -e OmslModelRepository=C:\temp\name The OmslModelRepository envi-
ronment variable can be used to specify the directory for saving models.
[PATH]\hmc.exe -e OMSL_COMPANY_SETTINGS=[PATH] Access to company-wide de-
fault settings for the software.
Registry parameters
Each version of the software manages its own registry parameters in a branch under
[HKEY_CURRENT_USER \ Software \ OPEN MIND \ [Software]].
CloseBeforeLoad There are two ways of opening a document. You can specify to either
always open a document in a new program window (option = false as the default setting) or
to always open a document in the same program window. In this case, any open document
will be closed first (option = true).
Language = [language code] The language for the software can be changed. Enter a
language code to do so.
LastRecentFiles Clear the list of recently used documents by deleting this key.
Information about undocked tabs is managed in the following parameters:
AdditionalWindows
auxGeometry
auxWindowDocks
auxWindowState
hyperCAD®-S
556

-- 570 of 600 --

Environment variables
HC_ADDITIONAL_HCCONFIG
Read in user-defined presets for user-defined commands *.xml.
Set up the Windows environment variable. Enter the HC_ADDITIONAL_HCCONFIG envi-
ronment variable under Variable name and enter the path, e.g. W:\Companyfolder\HCCON-
FIG under Variable value. The files located in the designated directory are evaluated in
alphabetical order.
Configure the availability of commands
Generate an additional toolbar or menu with command calls and specify in which software
based on
hyperCAD®-S these entries should be available. This is a licence-independent
procedure.
1. Adapt HC_ADDITIONAL_HCCONFIG
To do this, use the HC_ADDITIONAL_HCCONFIG Windows environment variable.
Select an existing directory or generate a new directory. Enter this directory in the
environment variable, for example, X:\server\cad\myconfig. The directory should not be
read-only.
2. Adapt HCCONFIG file
Copy an existing *.hcconfig.xml file to this directory and adapt it:
The ProgramName attribute can be used to include or exclude a software program.
To exclude, a ! must be placed in front. A combination of include and exclude is not
possible.
Example 27.
ProgramName=”hyperCAD-S” includes the display of the toolbar in this software pro-
gram. The toolbar will not be available in all other software programs.
ProgramName=”!hyperCAD-S” excludes the display of the toolbar in this software pro-
gram. The toolbar will be available in all other software programs.
The attribute can be used in <Command> and <Configuration>.
Write the software program names, for example, as follows: “hyperCAD-S”, “CAD
Viewer”, “hyperMILL SHOP Viewer”.
Once inserted, it may then look like this:
Example 28. Menu item for opening an Excel file, available only in
hyperCAD®-S
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="no"?>
<Configuration>
<Commands FormatVersion="1">
<Command Name="Start_excel01"
ProgramName="hyperCAD-S"
Menu="Special"
MenuText="Start excel file1..."
Tooltip="Start excel file..."
Statustip="Start excel file..."
Enabled="IsDocumentActive"
Icon="%MYDIR%/files/images/excel-in.png"
Run="RunScript">
Execute('C:/Program Files (x86)
/Microsoft Office
/root/Office16
/EXCEL.EXE',
'c:/temp/test.xlsx');
</Command>
